# next: a daemon that serves a tree of JSON nodes, for any client

## Contract
One daemon per session owns every terminal. Clients (TUI, phone, CLI) connect over one stream and exchange JSON objects, one per line (`t`-tagged). The state is a tree of **nodes** opened on demand: `session` (level 0: workspaces with a summary) → `workspace` (level 1: its panes) → `pane` (level 2: its screen). Every node has `id` (uuid), `v` (its document version), `epoch` (changes at every daemon start), `rev` (counts changes), and `attention`.

**The envelope never changes** (`hello`, `open`/`close`, `snapshot`/`patch`/`gone`, `cmd`/`reply`, `error`). Evolution happens in node documents: additive fields never bump `v` and receivers ignore unknown fields; a breaking change adds `v+1` of that one node type, served beside the old. At `hello` the client lists the versions it reads per node type (`accepts`) and the daemon answers the version it will serve (`accepted`); a type without a common version is not served. There is no global protocol number.

A client opens a node and gets a `snapshot`, then `patch`es (RFC 6902 subset `add`/`remove`/`replace`, `from`→`to` rev) until it closes it. A patch never edits `rev`: the client applies the ops to the cached document it holds at `from`, then sets `rev` to `to`; any other state means reopen for a snapshot. Opening with `epoch`+`since` resumes from a cache: the daemon replies one `patch` (empty when nothing changed) when the epoch matches and the revision is still retained, else a `snapshot`. The epoch sent is the one in the cached document, never the connection's. Clients cache nodes by `(epoch, id)`.

**Attention** is a typed item the daemon computes once and a client never re-derives: `question`, `plan`, `permission`, `shell_done` (with `exit_code`), `agent_done`, `screen`, `merged`, `ci_failed`. Each names the node that opens when tapped and the `rev` that raised it. A node's `attention` lists its whole subtree, so it bubbles to the session. Looking is shared: a client that shows a node sends `mark_seen {node, rev}` and the daemon ends the attentions that node raised up to that revision, so every client clears them (the user's decision; the first proposal was a per-client *seen*). Each client also keeps a local record so it clears at once and while offline. An attention lasts as long as its cause (a `shell_done` ends when the next command starts), so the daemon drops it then; whether a person looked at it is the client's *seen*. There is no approve/deny anywhere.

**Shell blocks.** A shell pane can also be read as the commands it ran. When the shell reports its marks (below), the pane carries `blocks`, the id of a `blocks` node (type `blocks`, v1, opened on demand like any node): the pane's commands, newest last, each with its folder, start time, duration, exit status and output. The output is rows of styled spans (text with colour and bold, italic, underline), resolved by the daemon, so a client draws spans and needs no terminal emulator. A command that takes the whole screen (nvim) is a block with `full` and no output, and the node is in mode `full` while it runs: clients then draw the pane's screen node instead. The designs are `docs/prototypes/tui.html` (terminal and phone). The shell's input stays with the shell: a client's editor sends the line with `write` (text, then `\r`), stops a program with `write` of `^C`, and repeating or copying a block is the client's business. While the shell waits for a line, the pane node also says what it holds, `input: {text, cursor}`, so a client can draw the line in a field of its own (the terminal client does, N63; the phone's mirroring of it is OPEN-6) without the shell's prompt: the daemon notes where the shell says its prompt ends (OSC 133;B) and reads the line from there on the pane's screen.

**Changes.** The changes of a workspace's git repository are two more node types, opened on demand like any other. `open_diff {workspace, scope}` replies `{node}`, the id of a `diff` node (v1) that lists the files a scope changes: `uncommitted` (the working tree against `HEAD`, with the untracked files), `general` (this branch against its merge base with `origin/<default branch>`) or `commit` (with a `commit` id: what that commit changed). `open_commits {workspace}` replies the id of a `commits` node (v1): the branch's commits, newest first. Each file carries its path, its status (`added`, `modified`, `deleted`, `renamed` with `old_path`, `untracked`), counts of added and deleted lines, and its hunks, each line prefixed `+`, `-` or a space as in a unified diff. The daemon only reads git and shapes its output; a client numbers the lines from the hunk's starts, marks the words that changed and colours the syntax. A binary file, or one the daemon did not read, is a file with no hunks, and a diff over 2 MiB lists paths only. Looking is shared here too: `set_viewed` records a file's `hash` for that workspace and scope, and every client shows its `seen` (`yes`, or `changed` when the file differs from what was looked at). While a client has an `uncommitted` node open the daemon reads it every 2 seconds and patches what changed, so an agent's edits arrive by themselves; the other scopes are read when opened and on `refresh_diff`. The designs are `docs/prototypes/tui-diff.html` (terminal) and the Cambios screens of `docs/prototypes/mobile.html` (phone); the viewer of files and Markdown is a separate part (OPEN-9).

**Agent attention.** An agent the daemon starts (`claude`) is given hooks for that run only (`--settings` with a file the daemon writes; it merges with the person's own hooks and writes nothing to their settings). Each hook runs `jwn hook <event>`, which tells the daemon with `agent_event` what the agent is doing, for the pane named by the environment. The daemon, not the hook, turns that into attention: a permission prompt is `permission`, a question or a plan awaiting approval is `question` or `plan`, a finished turn is `agent_done`, and the agent moving on (a tool ran, a prompt was sent, the session or the program ended) ends what it had raised. A `claude` typed at a prompt has no hooks (RISK-29).

**Pull requests.** For each worktree workspace with a pane, the daemon looks every minute at the newest pull request of its branch with `gh` (the person's own login; the daemon holds no token) and raises `merged` when it was merged, or `ci_failed` when it is open and a check failed. It raises only when what it sees changes, so a mark a person has looked at does not come back; the old jw looked at merges only (`⚑`) and had no failed-CI mark.

**Closing, removing and looks.** A client can close a pane (`close_pane`), close a workspace (`remove_workspace`) and, for a worktree workspace, remove the worktree with it; the terminal client and the phone do all three (the phone since N54, asking first). A worktree is never removed by force: the client reads the `uncommitted` changes first and, when there are any, lists them and offers only to close the workspace; the branch stays, so commits not yet pushed are safe. The look of each client (a **mode**: system, dark or light; and a **theme** for each kind, chosen from the ten palettes of `contract/themes.json`: five dark ones (One Dark, Catppuccin Mocha, Tokyo Night, Gruvbox Dark, Solarized Dark) and five light ones (One Light, Catppuccin Latte, Tokyo Day, Gruvbox Light, Solarized Light), each saying which it is, so a selector lists only the kind in force) is a setting of that client, not of the daemon: the terminal's is the file `settings.json`, the phone's is kept on the phone, and neither changes the other. Several sessions in one daemon are not built (RAT-16). The designs are `docs/prototypes/tui-manage.html` (terminal) and, in `docs/prototypes/mobile.html`, the screens *Ajustes: tema y modo* and *Cerrar un panel*.

**Project settings.** A repository may carry a `.jw.toml` at its root, versioned with the code and in the format the old jw wrote (an old file works as it is; what jwn does not use yet is ignored). The daemon reads it afresh each time it is needed and no client does: `root` (where worktrees go, `<repo>-wt` beside the repository by default), `setup` (shell commands run once in a new worktree), `open` (`shell` or `claude`: what the one panel of a workspace runs, N95; the old `[agent]`, `[layout]` and `editor` keys are ignored). A client asks `project_config {repo}` to show what a worktree will be, makes it with `create_worktree` and then asks `start_workspace {workspace}`, which starts the one panel: the setup runs in it first and then the shell stays or `claude` starts. Without a `.jw.toml` everything is as it was before N78.

**What agents use.** An agent running in a pane can read the session and act on it with the binary's own subcommands: `ls` and `pane ls` (what there is), `read` (a pane's screen, or the commands a shell pane ran and what they printed), `prompt` (type a line to another agent), `worktree` (make a worktree the way the terminal client does, with its `.jw.toml`, and optionally hand its agent a task), `pane open` and `pane close`, and `skill install`, which puts a skill that teaches the agent all this in the person's Claude configuration. They are clients of the daemon like any other: they open nodes and send the commands that exist, and the contract does not change. **A pane stays a shell and the agent is the program that runs in it** (RAT-35): a command finds an agent by the foreground program of a pane (`claude` or `codex`), whether a layout started it or a person typed it.

**The phone link from the terminal.** The terminal client turns the phone link on and pairs a phone itself (`m`), as `jwn pair` does: a box with the QR and the link, and the question of the six-digit code when a phone scans. The question comes to the person (the box opens by itself), and only `y`, pressed once the question has been on the screen for a moment, accepts. `jwn pair`, `jwn devices` and `jwn revoke` stay for use without the client. The design is `docs/prototypes/tui-phone.html`.

A **pane is a terminal**: its process is always the user's `$SHELL`; a program runs inside it (`running` and `last_program` carry it, and the pane's `title` is its name).

**A panel with a program.** `n` in the terminal client still makes a shell; `N` (after `^␣` from the terminal level) opens a menu, «panel nuevo», with the shell first and then the programs the person listed in `config.toml` (`claude` alone while there is no file): picking one asks the daemon for a pane that starts it, so the pane is still a shell that runs the program first and is a shell again when it ends. The list is the terminal client's own, as `settings.json` is (RAT-20); the daemon and the phone do not read it (the phone's `+` stays a shell, REQ-234). The design is the menu of `docs/prototypes/tui-levels.html`.

**The conversation** (N81 to N86; prototype `docs/prototypes/conversation.html`; what the engine says and takes, `docs/claude-engine.md`). When the person types `claude` in a pane, the shell integration runs the binary's own `claude` instead of the original (a function of the interactive shell, so a script, `command claude` and the full path still run the original). The binary asks the daemon for a **conversation** (`open_conversation`) and stays in the pane's foreground, so the pane is still a shell that runs a program (RAT-35, RAT-38). The daemon runs Claude Code as an **engine**, headless and speaking JSON, and turns what it says into one node, `conversation`, that every client draws its own way: the terminal client in place of the pane's screen, the phone flat, any other terminal as lines (the binary prints a plain version into the pane's own terminal). The engine belongs to the daemon and not to a client (RAT-36): it goes on when no client is connected, and a phone can approve what a terminal started. There is no Claude theme to set: every colour is the client's.

What the node holds: the person's messages and Claude's (the text growing as it streams), each tool as a line with its result and, for an edit, its diff, subagents as cards, what runs in the background (subagents, shells, monitors, loops), Claude's checklist, the pages it published, and at most one **question** waiting for the person (a permission to use a tool, a choice Claude asks for, or a plan to approve), which the first answer from any client settles. Messages typed while Claude works wait in a queue the daemon keeps. The conversation has a **name** (the first words of the first message until the person renames it) and an **accent**, one of the theme's six colours, which are the conversation's and not Claude's (RAT-41); the **model**, the **effort** and the **mode** (`manual`, `accept edits`, `plan`, `auto`) can change while it goes on. Attention comes from the engine's own events (a permission or a choice waits, a turn is done, including one a monitor started), so a conversation uses no hook. The terminal client's footer shows the mode at the left, `⚙ n` while something runs in the background and the model at the right, and nothing else (RAT-42).

What jwn does because the engine does not without its screen (all tested, `docs/claude-engine.md`): `!command` is a shell line (the engine sends it to the model as text); `/help`, `/status`, `/resume`, `/tasks`, `/artifacts`, `/skills`, `/permissions` and `/memory` are screens the clients draw (the engine answers that they are not available); `/cost` is the conversation's cost (the engine prints the subscription's use); a command or skill is sent as the line it is, and the client shows the command and not what it expands to. A command that needs Claude's own screen (`/login`, `/vim`, `/doctor`…) says so and points at `command claude`, which runs the original as today.

**The Markdown viewer** (N88 to N90; prototype `docs/prototypes/markdown.html`). A read-only screen that draws a Markdown document: opened from the plan Claude asks to approve (`v` in the question's box on the terminal, a button on the phone) and from a `.md` of the file tree; every other file still opens in the editor. The daemon reads and parses the document once (`pulldown-cmark`) and replies with **blocks**, so both clients draw the same document their own way and neither parses Markdown (RAT-45). The plan comes whole from the daemon (the engine gave it whole; the box keeps its first lines), and from the viewer it is answered with the same three answers. The viewer is not an editor: `e` opens the file in the person's editor.

## Interfaces
Home of every shape is `contract/schema/contract.schema.json`; the examples are `contract/fixtures/{client,daemon,phone,bridge,invalid}`; the Rust types are `crates/contract/src/lib.rs`. Do not restate them here. The phone link adds `PhoneMsg` and `BridgeMsg` (`crates/contract/src/link.rs`). The QR link is `jwn://<ipv4>:<port>/<token>?fp=<64 hex>` for the private-address listener, `jwn://<tunnel host>/<token>` for the tunnel, and with both `jwn://<ipv4>:<port>/<token>?fp=<64 hex>&t=<tunnel host>`.

| Direction | `t` | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| client→daemon | `hello` | `client`, `accepts: {type: [v]}` |
| | `open` / `close` | `node`, optional `epoch`+`since` to resume |
| | `cmd` | `id`, `cmd` (below) |
| daemon→client | `hello` | `epoch`, `root` (session id), `accepted: {type: v}` |
| | `snapshot` | a whole `node` |
| | `patch` | `node`, `epoch`, `from`, `to`, `ops` |
| | `gone` | node removed or unknown |
| | `reply` | `id`, `ok` + `result?` or `error{code,message}` |
| | `error` | a message with no `id` to reply to |

The `blocks` node: `pane`, `mode` (`blocks` or `full`), `blocks`: `Block {id, cmd, cwd, started, running, code?, ms?, truncated?, full?, interrupted?, restart?, out}`, `out` = rows of `Span {t, fg?, bg?, b?, i?, u?}`, colours a palette index (0 to 255) or `#rrggbb`. Everything is in `contract/schema/contract.schema.json`; examples in `contract/fixtures/daemon/{snapshot-blocks,snapshot-blocks-full,patch-blocks-append}.json`.

The pane's `input` (N63): `{text, cursor}`, optional and additive (a pane node of v1 gains it, nothing is bumped): `text` is what the shell holds after its prompt, the rows a wrap of the screen split joined and a hard line break as `\n`; `cursor` is the cursor's place in `text` in characters. It is absent while a command runs and for a shell that does not report its prompt (`crates/daemon/src/pty.rs`, `input_of`). A client of an older daemon sees no `input` and draws the screen's line instead.

Layout (N72): a `workspace` node gains `layout`, optional and additive: a tree whose leaves are exactly the node's `panes`, `{t: "pane", pane}` or `{t: "split", dir: "row"|"col", ratio, a, b}` (`row` puts `a` on the left and `b` on the right, `col` puts `a` above; `ratio` is the share of `a` in thousandths, 100 to 900). It is absent while the workspace has no panes and from a daemon that does not keep one, whose clients arrange the panes themselves. `new_pane` gains `beside` (a pane of the workspace) and `dir` (`row` when absent).

The `diff` node: `workspace`, `scope` (`uncommitted`, `general`, `commit`), `commit?`, `base?` (`origin/main`, for `general`), `state` (`ok`, or `no_base`), `too_large?`, `omitted?` (files left out of a too large diff), `files`: `DiffFile {path, old_path?, status, binary?, unreadable?, added, deleted, hash, seen?, hunks}`, `Hunk {context, old_start, old_lines, new_start, new_lines, lines}` with each line `+text`, `-text` or ` text`. The `commits` node: `workspace`, `base?`, `more?`, `commits`: `{sha, short, subject, time}` (time in Unix seconds). Examples in `contract/fixtures/daemon/{snapshot-diff*,snapshot-commits}.json`; the schema is home.

Commands (`cmd`): `rename_session`, `rename_workspace`, `rename_pane`, `swap_panes`, `grow_pane`, `list_files`, `find_files`, `sync_workspace`, `open_folder`, `list_dir`, `create_worktree`, `remove_workspace`, `new_pane`, `close_pane`, `write`, `resize`, `mark_seen`, `open_diff`, `open_commits`, `refresh_diff`, `set_viewed`, `agent_event`, and the phone link's `mobile_start`, `mobile_link`, `mobile_wait`, `mobile_answer`, `mobile_stop` (REQ-84). Error codes: `bad_request`, `not_found`, `unsupported`, `conflict`, `git_failed`, `io`.

Command semantics (N3): `open_folder` takes an absolute path of an existing directory and opens it once (`conflict` if open; `not_found`, `bad_request` for a missing path, a file or a relative path). `create_worktree` makes `<repo parent>/<repo name>-wt/<branch with / → ->` with `git worktree add` (a new branch from `base`, or an existing branch checked out, never both) and opens it as `<repo>: <branch>`; names that are options or not branch names are `bad_request`, git's own message is `git_failed`. `remove_workspace` closes a workspace; with `remove_worktree` it first runs `git worktree remove` without force, so a worktree with changes stays and so does its workspace. A successful open replies `result: {workspace, path}`.

Panes (N4): `new_pane` starts the user's `$SHELL` (else `/bin/sh`) in the workspace folder on a PTY (24×80 until `resize`, at most 500 each) with `TERM=xterm-256color` and `JWN_PANE_ID` set (`identity::env_var`), and replies `result: {pane}`. The pane node's `screen` is the vt100 screen as text rows (`lines`) and, while the program is on the alternate screen, the same rows with their colours (`spans`, N98: rows of the `Span` of the `blocks` node). A shell that exits takes its pane with it, like closing a terminal; `close_pane` and `remove_workspace` end the shells. After a daemon restart the saved panes get a fresh shell with the same ids (the screen and scrollback are not kept). A shell that prints OSC 133 marks (`ESC ] 133 ; D ; <code> BEL` finished, `ESC ] 133 ; C BEL` command started) raises `shell_done` with the exit code, which the next command start ends; shell integration scripts that print them for zsh, bash and fish come later.

Changes (N32): `open_diff` and `open_commits` take a workspace whose folder is inside a git repository and reply `result: {node}`; the same workspace and scope give the same node while it exists. The daemon runs the user's own `git` (`crates/daemon/src/diff.rs`) with no pager, no colour, no external diff or textconv and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`. `uncommitted` is `diff -M HEAD` (against the empty tree before the first commit) plus the files of `ls-files --others --exclude-standard`, read from disk (a symlink, or a file over 512 KiB, is `unreadable`; a file with a NUL in its first 8000 bytes is `binary`). `general` is `diff -M --merge-base origin/<base> HEAD`, with `<base>` taken from `origin/HEAD` (`no_base` when that is not set). `commit` is `show -M -m --first-parent --format=` of the id. The commits are `log -n 201` of `origin/<base>..HEAD`, or of `HEAD` with no base. Every read runs at the top of the repository the workspace is in, so a workspace that is a subfolder shows the repository's changes. A commit named by 4 to 40 hex digits is resolved to its full id, which is the node's `commit`, so one commit is one node however it was named. A file's `hash` is the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of its path, status, rename source and lines. `set_viewed {node, path, viewed}` is `not_found` for a node, or a path in it, that is not there. A node nobody has opened 60 seconds after its creation, or after the last client closed it, is removed.

Agent hooks (N36): `agent_event {pane, event, notification_type?, tool?}` with `event` one of `notification`, `pre_tool_use`, `post_tool_use`, `user_prompt_submit`, `stop`, `session_end`. The settings file is `<state>/claude-hooks.json` (`crates/daemon/src/launch.rs`): `Notification` is asked for only `permission_prompt|elicitation_dialog|agent_needs_input` and `PreToolUse` only for `AskUserQuestion|ExitPlanMode`; the other hooks are asked for always, with a 5 s timeout. What each event raises (`crates/daemon/src/agents.rs`):

| Event | Ends first | Raises |
|---|---|---|
| `notification` `permission_prompt` | | `permission` (not while a `plan` or `question` is pending) |
| `notification` `elicitation_dialog`, `agent_needs_input` | | `question` |
| `pre_tool_use` `AskUserQuestion` | permission, question, plan, agent_done | `question` |
| `pre_tool_use` `ExitPlanMode` | the same | `plan` |
| `post_tool_use`, `user_prompt_submit`, `session_end` | the same | |
| `stop` | the same | `agent_done` |

Looks (N38): `settings.json` in `identity::config_dir()` is `{"mode": "system"|"dark"|"light", "dark": <id of a dark theme>, "light": <id of a light theme>}`, all optional: the mode picks the kind (`system` follows the machine) and the kind picks which of the two to draw with, so changing the mode brings back the theme last picked for it. The ten palettes are `contract/themes.json` (read by Rust through `contract::themes`; the phone's table follows in N41): per theme (`id`, `name`, `kind`: `dark` or `light`) the twelve colours `bg panel fg dim line sel blue green yellow red magenta cyan`, the ones `docs/prototypes/tui-manage.html` and `mobile.html` draw with. Closing and removing use the commands that exist: `close_pane {pane}`, `remove_workspace {workspace, remove_worktree?}` (N3), and `open_diff {workspace, scope: uncommitted}` to know whether a worktree is clean; no message changes.

Phone link in the terminal client (N42): `m` asks the daemon with `mobile_start` (reply `{uri, lan?, tunnel?, pending}`: at once over the network address while the tunnel opens in the daemon, N48), asks again with `mobile_link` until `pending` is false, listens with `mobile_wait` in slices of 2 seconds so that a stop is heard, answers with `mobile_answer` and stops with `mobile_stop`; all from a thread of its own over its own connection (`crates/jwn/src/tui/phone.rs`), because a link with no private address waits for the tunnel for up to 90 seconds. The QR is the link at the lowest level that fits, two rows of modules to a terminal line, drawn black on white with a two-module quiet zone whatever the theme.

Opening and making (N49): `create_worktree` takes an optional `folder`, the name of the worktree's folder inside `<repo>-wt/` (one path component, at most 80 characters, no control character; else `bad_request`); without it the folder is the branch with its slashes made dashes, as before. The terminal client's `w` sends the name it asked for as the folder and `feat/<name>` as the branch, from the branch of the workspace the person is in (`base`). Both `o` and `w` open the workspace they make with `new_pane`, and the client opens it as soon as the session shows it (`crates/jwn/src/tui/open.rs`).

Project settings (N78, `crates/daemon/src/project.rs`): `project_config {repo}` (`repo` is any path inside a repository) replies `result: {source?, root, setup, open}`: `source` is the `.jw.toml` read (left out when there is none), `root` the absolute folder worktrees go in, `setup` the commands as written and `open` (`shell` or `claude`) what the one panel runs. `start_workspace {workspace, open?}` (`open` overrides the settings') replies `result: {panes: [id]}` with the one panel's id. The file's keys: `root` (`~` is the home; a relative path starts at the repository's root), `setup` (a list of shell lines; markers `{name}`, the worktree's folder name, and `{branch}`, its branch, each already quoted for the shell), and `open` (`shell` or `claude`, `shell` by default). All other keys, `[agent]`, `[layout]` and `editor` of old files among them, are ignored. `create_worktree` puts the worktree in `<root>/<folder>`. The phone's worktree sheet reads the same answer (N79, REQ-347 to REQ-352).

The agents' commands (N80, `crates/jwn/src/agent.rs`): the subcommands are `ls [--json]`, `pane ls <ws> [--json]`, `pane open <ws> [--split right|down] [--of <pane>] [--] [<program words>…]` (no words: a shell; it prints the new pane's id), `pane close <ws> <pane> [--force]`, `read <ws> [--pane <p>] [--lines N] [--blocks]`, `prompt <ws> [--pane <p>] [--tag] [--now] [--] <text>`, `worktree <name> [--in <repo>] [--branch <b>] [--from <ref>] [--task <text>]` and `skill [install]`. `<ws>` is a workspace's id, or exactly its name, its branch or its folder's name, or else the unique workspace whose name or branch starts with it; `<p>` is a pane's id, an id's unique prefix or its title. An agent's place is the environment variable `identity::env_var("PANE_ID")` that every pane has. `ls` reads only the `session` node; a pane's foreground program is its `running.argv` (a `pane` node), so `pane ls`, `read` and `prompt` open the workspace's panes. Exit codes: 0, 1 (an error, written to stderr as `<bin> <command>: <message>`), 2 (bad use). `identity::env_var("AGENT_WAIT_MS")` shortens the 40 seconds the commands wait for an agent, for tests only.

Browsing the PC's folders (N50): `list_dir {path?, hidden?}` takes an absolute path (the daemon's `$HOME` when none) and replies `result: {path, parent?, home, dirs: [{name, git}], total}`: `path` is the canonical folder, `parent` is left out at `/`, `home` is the daemon's home (clients write it as `~`), `dirs` are its subfolders (links to folders included) sorted without regard to case, the first 500 of the `total`, `git` when the subfolder holds a `.git`. Names starting with a dot are left out unless `hidden`. A path that is relative or not a folder is `bad_request`, a missing one `not_found`, one the daemon cannot read `io`. A session's workspace summary carries `project` the same as its workspace node: the repository of a worktree, and the folder's own path when the folder is a repository's root (N52), so a client can tell which of its workspaces are repositories and make a worktree of one it has not opened (`crates/daemon/src/browse.rs`, `crates/daemon/src/core.rs`). The `session` node lists its workspaces grouped (`crates/daemon/src/grouping.rs`, REQ-219): clients draw the list as it comes.

Programs for a new panel (N60): `config.toml` in `identity::config_dir()` holds one `[[program]]` table per entry: `name` (the menu's label), `command` (a list of strings: the program and its arguments, sent as `new_pane`'s `program`) and optionally `key` (one lowercase letter). Unknown keys are ignored. The terminal client reads it (`crates/jwn/src/tui/programs.rs`) and never writes it; `new_pane` and the pane node do not change.

Entering (N46): `jwn` with no arguments, or `jwn tui`, is the terminal client. It connects to the daemon on the socket; when none answers it starts `jwn daemon` itself in the background (`crates/jwn/src/main.rs`), with no terminal to read from, its output in `<state>/daemon.log` and a process group of its own so that the hang-up of the terminal does not reach it, and waits up to 5 seconds for it.

Development link: with `JWN_TCP=127.0.0.1:7870` the daemon also listens on loopback TCP, and `adb reverse tcp:7870 tcp:7870` lets a phone on a cable reach it; any process on the machine can use it, so it is off by default and prints a warning. The phone gets pairing, TLS and the tunnel later.

The phone link (N22–N27): a **bridge** the daemon hosts (crate `web`) lets a phone reach the daemon away from a cable. The phone opens a WebSocket to the bridge (loopback, fronted by a Cloudflare quick tunnel that ends TLS; optionally also a TLS listener on the PC's private address with a self-signed certificate the phone pins), sends `pair` (first time) or `auth` (afterwards), and once the bridge answers `ready` every WebSocket text message is one daemon line, both ways. The bridge connects to the daemon's own socket only after the phone is authenticated. Pairing: `jwn pair` makes a one-use token and prints a QR link; the phone sends it with its name and a code it shows; the person at that terminal compares the code and answers `y`; only then a device secret is issued (the PC keeps its hash). `jwn devices` and `jwn revoke` manage them without a daemon.

Transport: JSON lines on a Unix socket (mode 0600, in a 0700 dir) whose path comes from `identity::socket_path()` (`crates/identity/src/lib.rs`). `session.json` in `identity::state_dir()` is private to the daemon (written whole, atomically; a file it cannot read is moved to `session.json.bad`, never overwritten). Client side: `crates/wire` (`Client` keeps the node cache and applies patches). The phone later carries the same objects one per WebSocket text message.

The `conversation` node (N81, v1): `pane`, `session?` (the engine's id), `name`, `accent` (`blue green yellow red magenta cyan`), `model`, `effort`, `mode` (`manual`, `acceptEdits`, `plan`, `auto`), `status` (`idle`, `resuming`, `thinking`, `streaming`, `tool`, `asking`, `compacting`, `dead`), `since?` (when the turn that runs began), `first` (the index of `items[0]`: the node keeps the last 300), `items`, `ask?`, `queue` (the texts waiting), `tasks`, `todos`, `artifacts`, `commands`, `cost`, `limits?`. An item is `{t: user|text|tool|agent|shell|out|note|result|divider, …}`: `user {text, images?}`, `text {text, live?}`, `tool {id, name, arg, state: run|ask|ok|denied|failed, summary?, output?, diff?, url?, by?}`, `agent {id, name, description, state, tools, tokens, ms, last?, steps}`, `shell {command, state, output}`, `out {lines}`, `note {text, tone?}`, `result {ms, tools, cost}`, `divider {text, kind: restart|compact|clear|resume}`. `ask` is `{id, kind: permission|question|plan, tool, title, command?, diff?, plan?, options: [{key, label}], multi?}`; a task is `{id, kind: agent|bash|monitor|loop|other, label, state: run|done|stopped|failed, started, info?}`; a todo `{text, done}`; an artifact `{title, url}`; a command `{name, source: jwn|claude|project|skill|plugin, kind: local|panel|text|prompt|screen, description?}`. Text grows by `replace` of `/items/<n>/text`; everything else is an `add` or a `replace` of the field it changes. The pane gains `conversation?` (additive, nothing is bumped). Home: the schema.

The commands of the conversation (N81 on): `open_conversation {pane, resume?, args?, env?}` (reply `{node, session}`: `args` are the flags `claude` was typed with, `env` the environment it was typed in, which the engine runs with; no path of a program travels), `conversation_send {node, text, images?}` (`images`: `[{media_type, data}]`, base64, at most four), `conversation_answer {node, ask, key, text?, by?}` (`by` says where the answer comes from), `conversation_interrupt {node}`, `conversation_set {node, name?, accent?, model?, effort?, mode?}`, `conversation_shell {node, command}`, `conversation_stop_task {node, task}`, `conversation_close {node}`, `list_conversations {workspace}` (reply `[{id, name, updated}]`) and `conversation_permissions {node}` (the rules of the person's settings files, read only). The attention kinds `permission`, `question`, `plan` and `agent_done` are the ones that exist.

The commands of the viewer (N88): `read_markdown {workspace, path}` (a Markdown file of the workspace, `path` relative to its folder or absolute) and `read_plan {node, ask}` (the plan that waits in a conversation); both reply a `MarkdownDoc`: `{title, path?, truncated, blocks}`, where `title` is the first heading or the file's name and `path` the file (a plan's, when the engine said it). A **block** is `{t: heading, level, text}`, `{t: para, text}`, `{t: quote, blocks}`, `{t: list, start?, items: [{task?, blocks}]}` (`start` when it is numbered, `task` true or false for a checkbox), `{t: code, lang, text}`, `{t: table, head, rows}` (each cell is text) or `{t: rule}`; **text** is a list of `{t, b?, i?, code?, href?}`. An image is the text `[imagen: …]` and raw HTML is text. The first reader is the terminal client; the phone's is N90.

`start_workspace` also starts a folder's panel (N94, N95): the `open` of the settings its path has (the repository's when it is the top level of one, else its own `.jw.toml`, else its personal file named by the path); never its setup, ports or copies.

The settings also have `ports` (N93): names with offsets 0–99; a new worktree gets a slot and a block from 20000 + 100 × slot, and its panes `<PREFIX>_PORT_BASE` and `<PREFIX>_PORT_<NAME>`; `create_worktree` also replies `ports` (name to port) and `project_config` lists the settings' `ports`.

The settings also have `copy` (N92): paths of the checkout copied into each new worktree; `create_worktree` replies `{workspace, path, copied: [path], skipped: [{path, why}]}` (the last two only when there were some).

Editing the settings (N97, RAT-48): `edit_project_config {repo, to, set}` (`to` is `repo` or `personal`; `set` is `{root?, setup?, copy?, ports?, open?}`, `ports` a map of names to offsets) replies `{path, shadowed}` as `write_project_config` does; `root_text` of `project_config` is the `root` key as the file wrote it, left out when it has none. The terminal client's key is `p` (workspaces and panels), the phone's a «configuración del proyecto» in the sheet of a repository's workspace or a worktree.

The worktrees that exist (N99, RAT-50): `list_worktrees {repo}` replies `result: {worktrees: [WorktreeInfo]}` and `open_worktree {repo, path}` replies like `create_worktree`. `repo` is any path inside the repository, or inside one of its worktrees. A `WorktreeInfo` is `{path, branch?, head, missing?, changes?, workspace?}` (`crates/contract/src/lib.rs`, `contract/fixtures/daemon/reply-ok-worktrees.json`): `branch` is left out for a detached head, `head` is the first seven digits of the commit, `missing` says the folder is not there, `changes` is how many entries `git status --porcelain` lists (an untracked folder is one), and `workspace` is the id of the workspace that has it open. The list is what `git worktree list --porcelain` says without the main worktree, and the status of the worktrees is read up to eight at a time. `Workspace.adopted` (not in the contract, saved in `session.json`) marks a workspace made by `open_worktree`.

Where a project's settings live (N91, RAT-46): `project_config {repo}` replies `{source?, from?, personal, root, root_text?, setup, open, copy?, ports?}` (`from` is `repo` or `personal`, `personal` the path of the personal file) and `write_project_config {repo, to, text}` (`to` is `repo` or `personal`) replies `{path, shadowed}`. A repository's own `.jw.toml` is the team's and is never made by jwn on its own; the personal file is the person's, outside the repository, so a project that needs nothing needs no file.

## Acceptance
- REQ-1  THE contract SHALL define every message and node as JSON Schema in `contract/schema/contract.schema.json`.
- REQ-2  THE contract crate SHALL parse every fixture in `fixtures/client` and `fixtures/daemon` and write back the identical JSON.
- REQ-3  THE contract crate SHALL validate every such fixture against the schema.
- REQ-4  IF a fixture in `fixtures/invalid` passes the schema, THEN the conformance suite SHALL fail.
- REQ-5  IF the schema has a message type, command, patch op, node type, attention kind or error code with no valid fixture, THEN the conformance suite SHALL fail.
- REQ-6  WHEN a document carries a field the receiver does not know, the receiver SHALL ignore it.
- REQ-7  THE envelope SHALL NOT change; a breaking change SHALL add a new `v` of one node type.
- REQ-8  WHEN a client sends `hello`, the daemon SHALL answer `accepted` with the highest version in common per node type and omit a type with none.
- REQ-9  WHEN a client opens a node with the daemon's `epoch` and a retained `since`, the daemon SHALL send one `patch`; otherwise a `snapshot`.
- REQ-10 WHEN a node's attention changes, the daemon SHALL patch it and every ancestor a client has open.
- REQ-11 THE daemon SHALL compute attention; a client SHALL NOT derive it.
- REQ-12 THE fixtures SHALL be plain files with no Rust dependency, so a Kotlin suite can read the same ones.
- REQ-13 THE product name, env prefix and paths SHALL come from `identity.toml` only.
- REQ-14 WHEN a pane starts, its process SHALL be the user's `$SHELL`.
- REQ-15 IF a daemon is already listening on the socket, THEN a second daemon SHALL refuse to start; a socket nobody listens on SHALL be cleared.
- REQ-16 WHEN the daemon restarts, the session id and workspaces SHALL be the same and the epoch new.
- REQ-17 IF a line is not a contract message, or comes before `hello`, THEN the daemon SHALL send `error` and keep the connection.
- REQ-19 WHEN `open_folder` names an existing directory not yet open, the daemon SHALL add a `folder` workspace and patch the session node.
- REQ-20 WHEN `create_worktree` succeeds, the daemon SHALL have a git worktree beside the repository and a `worktree` workspace for it.
- REQ-21 IF a worktree has changes, THEN `remove_workspace` with `remove_worktree` and without `force` SHALL fail with `git_failed` and change nothing (REQ-282).
- REQ-22 WHEN a workspace is removed, the daemon SHALL send `gone` for its node to whoever has it open.
- REQ-23 WHEN `new_pane` succeeds, the daemon SHALL have a live PTY running the shell in the workspace folder and a pane node whose `screen` follows its output.
- REQ-24 WHEN the shell of a pane exits, the daemon SHALL remove the pane.
- REQ-25 WHEN a pane's shell prints a command-finished mark, the daemon SHALL raise `shell_done` with its exit code on that pane and every ancestor; WHEN the next command starts, it SHALL end it.
- REQ-26 WHEN a pane or its workspace is removed, the daemon SHALL end its shell.
- REQ-27 WHEN the daemon restarts, each saved pane SHALL get a new shell under the same id.
- REQ-28 WHILE no node a client has open changes, the daemon SHALL send that client nothing.
- REQ-29 WHEN an item is added to or removed from the end of a list in a node, the patch SHALL be one `add` or `remove` op for it, not the whole list.
- REQ-30 THE daemon SHALL be drivable with JSON alone through the `jwn` binary: start, commands, stop, restart, state restored (`crates/jwn/tests/e2e.rs`).
- REQ-31 THE Kotlin contract module SHALL parse every valid fixture, write back the identical JSON, validate it against the same schema, reject the invalid ones, and apply every `patch-cases` file as Rust does (`android/contract`, `ConformanceTest`).
- REQ-32 IF a fixture or the schema changes without the Kotlin types following, THEN the Kotlin suite SHALL fail (Gradle treats `contract/` as a test input).
- REQ-33 WHILE a workspace has attention its phone has not seen, its Home row SHALL show the mark of the most urgent one, with a count when there are several, and one line saying what it is.
- REQ-34 WHEN the person taps the line that says an attention in a Home row or an item in Avisos, the phone SHALL open the node the attention names (the pane's tab, not only the workspace) and mark it seen on that phone.
- REQ-35 THE phone SHALL keep a local seen record, node id to revision; it SHALL treat an attention as seen when its `rev` is not above the recorded one, and SHALL drop the record of a node from another epoch.
- REQ-36 THE phone SHALL NOT offer approve or deny controls on Home or Avisos.
- REQ-37 THE pane screens (claude, dev, shell) SHALL stay as in `docs/prototypes/mobile.html`.
- REQ-38 (withdrawn) The pane key row (`^␣ esc tab ↑ ↓ ← → ^C`) is removed for now; where those keys go is OPEN-5. The editor's stop button (`■`, `^C`) stays.
- REQ-39 THE Android client SHALL draw Home, Avisos and a workspace's panes from the shared fixtures, under the application id and label of `identity.toml`, beside any other jw app.
- REQ-40 THE phone's pane tabs and the terminal's pane rows SHALL be labelled with the pane's `title` (the program running in the shell, else the shell); a pane SHALL NOT have a role chosen at creation.
- REQ-41 WHEN a patch does not start at the revision a client holds, or its epoch differs, THEN the client SHALL drop the node and open it again for a snapshot.
- REQ-42 WHERE `<PREFIX>_TCP` names a loopback address, the daemon SHALL serve the same JSON lines there too; IF the address is not loopback, THEN it SHALL refuse to start.
- REQ-43 WHEN the Android client is started with `-e live host:port`, it SHALL draw that daemon's nodes, opening them on demand and retrying a lost connection, and SHALL write to the socket off the UI thread.
- REQ-44 THE terminal client SHALL be laid out as in `docs/prototypes/tui.html`: a 28-column sidebar of numbered workspaces (`●` with panes, `○` without, `↳` for a worktree) with the most urgent unseen mark at the right and the count when there are several, a header row with project, branch and path, each pane of the highlighted workspace as a rounded box (one fills the area, two side by side, from three the first takes the left half and the rest stack), the focused box with a blue border and its unseen attention in its top border, and a status bar with the mode (`MOVE`, `TERM`, `AVISOS`).
- REQ-45 WHILE the terminal client only highlights a workspace it SHALL preview it and mark nothing seen; WHEN Enter opens it, or `h`/`l` focuses a pane, it SHALL mark that workspace and pane seen; `a` SHALL open a box of what waits and Enter there SHALL go to the named pane and start typing in it; `n` SHALL ask the daemon for a new pane (REQ-175).
- REQ-46 WHEN a client sends `mark_seen {node, rev}`, the daemon SHALL end the attentions that node raised at or below `rev` and patch every client that has them; an attention raised later SHALL stay; an unknown node SHALL be `not_found`.
- REQ-47 WHEN the phone or the terminal shows the node an attention names, it SHALL send `mark_seen` for it, so the other client drops that attention too.
- REQ-48 WHILE a program other than the shell is in the foreground of a pane's terminal, the pane node SHALL carry it as `running` (its argv) with `title` its name (`nvim`, `claude`; an interpreter gives way to its script), and WHEN it ends `running` SHALL be absent, `last_program` SHALL hold it and `title` SHALL be the shell's name again.
- REQ-49 WHEN a workspace is opened on the phone before its document arrives, the phone SHALL select its first pane once it does.
- REQ-50 WHEN `new_pane` has a `program`, the daemon SHALL start the shell as `$SHELL -i -c '<program>; exec $SHELL -i'` with each word quoted for that shell, so the program receives its words as given and, when it ends, the pane is the shell it left; IF `program` is empty THEN it SHALL be `bad_request`.
- REQ-51 WHEN the program is an agent (`claude`) that names no conversation, the daemon SHALL start it with its own `--session-id <uuid>`.
- REQ-52 WHEN the daemon restarts, a pane whose agent was running with a known conversation that has a saved transcript SHALL start that agent again as `claude --resume <id>`; an agent that ended, or whose conversation has no transcript, SHALL NOT be started again, and the pane SHALL be a shell.
- REQ-53 THE terminal client SHALL take the mouse: a left click on a workspace row opens it, on a pane's box focuses it, on `avisos N` in the status bar opens the list, on an item of the list goes there, and outside the list closes it; the wheel over the sidebar or the list moves the highlight; a click anywhere else SHALL change nothing.
- REQ-54 WHEN a pane starts a shell with the integration (zsh, bash, fish), the pane node SHALL carry `blocks` from the start, the id of a `blocks` node that lists one block per command the shell ran, oldest first (none yet at first).
- REQ-55 WHILE a command runs its block SHALL have `running` true and no `code` or `ms`; WHEN it ends it SHALL carry `code`, `ms` and `running` false.
- REQ-56 THE output of a block SHALL be rows of spans with colours and attributes already resolved by the daemon, and SHALL NOT contain the prompt, the typed command or the marks.
- REQ-57 THE daemon SHALL keep at most 100 blocks per pane and 500 rows per block (the start of a longer output is dropped and the block marked `truncated`).
- REQ-58 WHILE a command uses the alternate screen, or a program that draws its own screen (claude, nvim, htop, … `procs::takes_screen`) is in the foreground, the node SHALL be in mode `full` and its block SHALL be `full` with no output kept; clients SHALL then draw the pane's screen.
- REQ-59 THE daemon SHALL give zsh, bash and fish a shell integration that reports, with OSC 133 (`A` prompt, `B` input, `C` output start, `D;<code>` end) and OSC 633 (`E;<base64 command>`, `P;Cwd=<base64 folder>`), what each command was, where it started and how it ended.
- REQ-60 WHEN a client's editor sends a line, it SHALL send it with `write` (the text and `\r`).
- REQ-61 WHILE a pane is open in the terminal client, every key SHALL go to its shell as the bytes a terminal sends (text, Enter, Backspace, Tab, Esc, the arrows, Home, End, Delete, PageUp, PageDown, and Ctrl and Alt combinations); `^␣` SHALL give the keyboard back to the client (the panel level, REQ-171) and Enter SHALL take it again.
- REQ-62 THE terminal client SHALL ask the focused pane to the size of its box with `resize`, once for each size.
- REQ-63 THE phone SHALL have an input line that sends its text and `\r` to the focused pane with `write`; it SHALL be disabled when the data are the fixtures.
- REQ-64 THE clients SHALL send what is typed without waiting for the daemon's reply.
- REQ-65 WHEN a shown pane has a `blocks` node in mode `blocks`, THE terminal client SHALL draw it inside the pane's box as blocks, the newest at the bottom next to the editor (folder and time, `❯` command, coloured output, a bar green, red or yellow by status) with the field of REQ-254 under them; WHEN the node is in mode `full` (but see REQ-259), or the pane has none, it SHALL draw the pane's screen.
- REQ-66 WHEN a block is clicked, THE terminal client SHALL select it and stop forwarding keys; WHILE one is selected, `c` SHALL copy its command and `o` its output (OSC 52), `r` SHALL write its command and `\r` to the pane, `j`/`k` SHALL move the selection, and Esc SHALL release it.
- REQ-67 WHILE the last block runs, THE editor SHALL show `■ ^C` (beside the `➤` since N63, REQ-255), and a click on it SHALL write `\u0003` to the pane (REQ-257).
- REQ-68 THE wheel over a pane drawn as blocks SHALL scroll its blocks, the newest at the bottom when not scrolled.
- REQ-69 WHEN the focused pane has a `blocks` node in mode `blocks`, THE phone SHALL draw it as flat blocks, the newest at the bottom: folder and time (`corriendo N s`, `salió N · t`), the command in bold, the output with its colours, a red bar and tint for a failure, a yellow one while it runs; WHEN the mode is `full`, or the pane has none, it SHALL draw the pane's screen (with a notice in the first case).
- REQ-70 WHEN a block is tapped, THE phone SHALL pick it and show Comando, Salida and Repetir; Repetir SHALL `write` its command and `\r` to the pane, and tapping the block again SHALL let it go.
- REQ-71 THE phone's editor SHALL show chips (folder and `⎇ branch`, or `◌ program` while the last block runs); WHILE it runs, a `■` button SHALL `write` `\u0003`.
- REQ-72 THE bridge SHALL accept WebSockets only on loopback and on a private IPv4 address (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16), never on `0.0.0.0`, and SHALL refuse a request that carries an `Origin` header.
- REQ-73 THE bridge SHALL NOT connect to the daemon for a connection until its `auth` succeeded or its `pair` was confirmed.
- REQ-74 THE pairing token SHALL be 128 random bits, valid 120 seconds, and spent the first time it is presented, whatever the answer.
- REQ-75 WHEN a phone presents a valid pairing token, `jwn pair` SHALL show its name and code and issue a device only on `y`; IF no answer comes in 60 seconds, or `jwn pair` is not running, THEN the bridge SHALL send `denied` (`timeout` or `declined`).
- REQ-76 THE device secret SHALL be 256 random bits shown to the phone once; the PC SHALL keep only its SHA-256 in `<state>/<pairing_store of identity.toml>` (mode 0600) and compare in constant time.
- REQ-77 IF an address fails 5 times in 60 seconds, THEN the bridge SHALL answer `locked_out` to it for the next 60 seconds; on the tunnel the address is `Cf-Connecting-Ip`, and a tunnel request without it SHALL be refused.
- REQ-78 WHEN a device is revoked (`jwn revoke`), the bridge SHALL refuse its secret and close its connections within 2 seconds; `jwn devices` and `jwn revoke` SHALL work with no daemon running.
- REQ-79 WHEN a phone is authenticated, the bridge SHALL send `ready` and then relay each WebSocket text message as one daemon line and each daemon line as one text message; IF a message is over 1 MiB, THEN the bridge SHALL close the connection.
- REQ-80 WHERE `cloudflared` is installed, THE daemon SHALL start a quick tunnel to the bridge's loopback port when the link is turned on, offer its host name only after the name resolves, and keep it until the link is turned off (REQ-190).
- REQ-81 WHERE the PC has a private IPv4 address, the bridge SHALL also listen there with TLS, using a self-signed certificate kept at `<state>/link-cert.pem` and reused across starts; the QR SHALL carry its SHA-256 as `fp`, and the phone SHALL connect to that address only if the certificate's SHA-256 equals the `fp` it stored.
- REQ-82 WHEN the addresses change, and after `ready`, the bridge SHALL send `endpoints`; the phone SHALL accept tunnel hosts under `trycloudflare.com` only and send its device secret only to a host it stored or one `endpoints` named over an authenticated connection.
- REQ-86 WHEN the bridge lets a phone in, `ready` SHALL carry the PC's name (`host`, optional, at most 64 characters).
- REQ-87 THE phone SHALL keep every PC it paired with, and SHALL remove one only when the person forgets it (not when the PC is off, unreachable or says it no longer knows the phone); WHILE its list of PCs is open it SHALL look at each PC every 10 seconds by authenticating and hanging up at `ready`, and show it as online (green), checking, off or no longer paired.
- REQ-88 WHEN the app opens and the phone has PCs, it SHALL connect to the one used last; WHEN the person connects to another from the list, the phone SHALL keep the current connection until the new one is `ready`.
- REQ-89 WHEN the daemon starts with devices already paired, it SHALL open the link again (the bridge and the private-address listener, on the port recorded in `<state>/link-port` if it is free, then the tunnel in the background) without `jwn pair`.
- REQ-90 WHILE the PC the phone opened on does not answer and the phone has not reached it since it opened, the phone SHALL say that it cannot find that PC by name, keep trying, offer to try now and to open the list of PCs, and enter by itself when the PC answers.
- REQ-91 WHEN `open_diff` names a workspace and a scope (`uncommitted`, `general`, or `commit` with a `commit` id), the daemon SHALL reply with the id of a `diff` node listing the files of that scope, the same id for the same workspace and scope while the node exists.
- REQ-92 THE scopes SHALL be: `uncommitted`, the working tree against `HEAD` (an empty tree before the first commit) plus the untracked files git does not ignore; `general`, `HEAD` against its merge base with `origin/<default branch>`; `commit`, what that commit changed against its first parent.
- REQ-93 IF a workspace's folder is not inside a git repository, or a commit id is not 4 to 40 hexadecimal digits, THEN `open_diff` and `open_commits` SHALL fail with `bad_request`; IF git fails, THEN with `git_failed` carrying git's message; and no node SHALL be left behind.
- REQ-94 WHEN `origin` has no default branch, a `general` node SHALL have `state` `no_base`, no files and no `base`.
- REQ-95 THE daemon SHALL give each file its path, `old_path` for a rename, status, counts of added and deleted lines, `hash` and hunks of lines prefixed `+`, `-` or a space; a binary file SHALL have `binary` and no hunks, and a file the daemon did not read SHALL have `unreadable` (the reason) and no hunks.
- REQ-96 IF the diff of a scope passes 2 MiB, THEN the node SHALL have `too_large`, list at most 200 files by path and status with no hunks and no `seen`, and give the number of files left out in `omitted`; `set_viewed` on it SHALL be `unsupported`.
- REQ-97 WHILE a client has an `uncommitted` node open, the daemon SHALL read it again every 2 seconds and patch it when it changed; THE daemon SHALL NOT run git for a node nobody has open, beyond reading it once when it is made.
- REQ-98 WHEN `refresh_diff` names a `diff` or `commits` node, the daemon SHALL read it again at once; an unknown node SHALL be `not_found`.
- REQ-99 WHEN `open_commits` names a workspace in a git repository, the daemon SHALL reply with the id of a `commits` node listing at most 200 commits of `origin/<base>..HEAD` (of `HEAD` when there is no base), newest first, each with its id, short id, subject and commit time, and `more` when older ones exist.
- REQ-100 WHEN `set_viewed {node, path, viewed}` names a file of a `diff` node, the daemon SHALL record the file's current `hash` for that workspace and scope (or forget it) and patch every client that has the node open; a file with a recorded `hash` equal to its own SHALL carry `seen` `yes`, one with a different `hash` SHALL carry `seen` `changed`, and one never marked SHALL carry none.
- REQ-101 THE recorded marks SHALL be kept in `session.json` across a restart; the daemon SHALL drop those of a removed workspace, and those of a file that is no longer in an `uncommitted` or `general` diff.
- REQ-102 THE daemon SHALL remove a `diff` or `commits` node that no client opened within 60 seconds of making it, or that no client has had open for 60 seconds, and SHALL send `gone` for those of a removed workspace.
- REQ-103 THE daemon SHALL run `git` for these reads without a pager, colour, external diff or text conversion, and without git's optional locks, so a read never blocks the user's own git.
- REQ-104 WHEN `d` is pressed on the highlighted workspace, THE terminal client SHALL open the diff viewer over that workspace's panes (the sidebar stays), ask the daemon for its `uncommitted` node and watch it; WHEN `esc` or `q` is pressed, or the sidebar is clicked, it SHALL close the viewer and stop watching the nodes it opened.
- REQ-105 THE viewer SHALL have the views Sin commitear, Commits and General (keys `1 2 3`, or a click on its tab), ask the daemon for each node once, and show how many files or commits each holds once it is known.
- REQ-106 WHILE the Commits view has no commit open, THE viewer SHALL list the commits (subject, short id, age) in the left column and `↵` SHALL open one; WHILE one is open, `( )` SHALL step to the previous or next commit and `h` SHALL go back to the list.
- REQ-107 THE viewer SHALL show the changed files as a tree on the left (a folder with a single folder in it as one row, `+ −` totals, a status letter M A D R ? and a mark when seen) and every file of the view in one scroll on the right, in the tree's order, with the current file's header pinned on top; a file that is seen SHALL be folded to its header.
- REQ-108 WHERE the body is 100 columns or wider, THE viewer SHALL draw changes side by side (a file that is new, untracked or deleted in one column), and below that in one column; `s` SHALL force one column, two, or go back to the width; WHERE the area is under 84 columns the tree SHALL be hidden and `tab` SHALL show it over the body.
- REQ-109 WHEN `v` is pressed, THE viewer SHALL send `set_viewed` for the current file (viewed unless it is already seen, then not) and, once the daemon has patched the node, go to the next file; `j`/`k` SHALL move between files, `]`/`[` between hunks, space and `b` by a page, `g`/`G` to the ends, and the wheel scroll the body.
- REQ-110 WHILE a view has no node yet THE viewer SHALL say it is reading; IF the daemon refuses or fails, THEN it SHALL show the daemon's message with the other views still reachable and `r` SHALL ask again; WHEN there is no base, no changes, or a diff over the limit, it SHALL say so (a diff over the limit lists names without counts).
- REQ-111 WHILE the `uncommitted` view is open THE viewer SHALL show `● en vivo`, and for 2.5 seconds after the daemon patches the node for a reason other than its own mark, `● actualizado`.
- REQ-112 THE viewer SHALL NOT put any control character of a diff, a path, a commit subject or an error message on the terminal: a tab becomes spaces and any other control character a dot.
- REQ-113 THE phone SHALL open the changes of a workspace from the `±` of its screen, on Sin commitear; a drop-down at the top SHALL change the workspace, and Back SHALL close the file, then the commit, then return to the workspace (REQ-218).
- REQ-114 THE phone SHALL show the views Sin commitear, Commits and General as tabs, ask the PC for each view's node once when first shown, and show the number of files or commits each holds once it is known (`!` when it failed).
- REQ-115 THE phone SHALL list the changed files as a tree (a folder with a single folder in it as one row, folding by tap, `+ −` totals, a status letter, a box at the right); tapping a file SHALL open it and tapping its box SHALL send `set_viewed` for it, unmarked when it is already seen.
- REQ-116 THE phone SHALL show a file as unified lines numbered from its hunks' starts, additions and deletions tinted and signed; a binary, unreadable or renamed file with no lines SHALL say so, and a file whose `seen` is `changed` SHALL carry the notice that it changed since it was looked at and a ring in place of the tick.
- REQ-117 WHILE the Commits view has no commit open THE phone SHALL list the commits (subject, short id, age); tapping one SHALL open its files with `‹ commits` and `◂ ▸` to the neighbouring commits.
- REQ-118 WHILE Sin commitear is open THE phone SHALL say `● en vivo` and how many files are seen, and for 2.5 seconds after the PC patches the node for a reason other than the phone's own mark it SHALL say `● actualizado`.
- REQ-119 WHILE a view has no node yet THE phone SHALL say it is reading; IF the PC refuses, fails or the link drops before it answers, THEN it SHALL show the message with `reintentar` and the other views reachable; WHEN there is no base, no changes or the diff is over the limit, it SHALL say so (names only, no counts, with the omitted number).
- REQ-120 WHEN the phone leaves the changes screen, it SHALL stop watching the nodes it opened (`close`), and a node it opened SHALL be opened again after the link comes back.
- REQ-121 THE phone SHALL tell the PC at `hello` that it reads `diff` and `commits` nodes; WHEN it has no PC (the fixtures), it SHALL serve the diff fixtures read-only.
- REQ-122 WHERE a pane's shell has no integration (an unsupported shell), the pane node SHALL carry no `blocks` and clients SHALL draw its screen.
- REQ-123 THE `blocks` node of a pane SHALL keep its id for as long as the pane lives.
- REQ-124 WHEN a pane is closed, or its workspace removed, the daemon SHALL send `gone` for its `blocks` node.
- REQ-125 THE daemon SHALL keep at most 4 MiB of block output per pane, by dropping the oldest blocks.
- REQ-126 WHERE a pane's shell is not zsh, bash or fish, the daemon SHALL start it unchanged.
- REQ-127 WHEN a client's editor stops a running program, it SHALL send `write` of `\u0003`.
- REQ-128 WHEN a pull request changes `next/`, CI SHALL run `just fmt-check` (Linux), `just clippy` and `just test` on Linux and macOS with zsh and fish installed, and `just android` (`.github/workflows/next.yml`).
- REQ-129 WHEN the daemon starts an agent (`claude`, by `new_pane` or by resuming it), it SHALL pass it `--settings <state>/claude-hooks.json`, a file it writes at start whose hooks run `<jwn> hook <event>`; an agent started with `--settings` of its own SHALL keep them and get no second set.
- REQ-130 WHEN `agent_event` arrives for a pane, the daemon SHALL raise on it: `permission` for a `notification` of `permission_prompt`; `question` for a `notification` of `elicitation_dialog` or `agent_needs_input`, and for a `pre_tool_use` of `AskUserQuestion`; `plan` for a `pre_tool_use` of `ExitPlanMode`; `agent_done` for `stop`; and nothing for any other notification or tool; an unknown pane SHALL be `not_found`.
- REQ-131 WHEN `agent_event` of any kind but `notification` arrives, the daemon SHALL first end the pane's `permission`, `question`, `plan` and `agent_done` attentions.
- REQ-132 THE `jwn hook <event>` command SHALL send `agent_event` for the pane named by `JWN_PANE_ID` with the `notification_type` and `tool_name` of the JSON on its stdin, and SHALL exit 0 without a word whatever fails (no pane, no daemon, unreadable input, an event it does not know).
- REQ-133 WHEN an agent's program ends, the daemon SHALL end the `permission`, `question`, `plan` and `agent_done` attentions of its pane.
- REQ-134 WHILE a `plan` or a `question` is pending on a pane, a `notification` of `permission_prompt` SHALL NOT raise `permission` on it.
- REQ-135 WHERE `gh` is found (on the path, or named by `JWN_GH`), THE daemon SHALL look every 60 seconds at the newest pull request of the branch of each worktree workspace that has a pane, with `gh pr list --head <branch> --state all --limit 1 --json number,state,statusCheckRollup` run in the worktree, outside the hub's lock and waiting at most 10 seconds.
- REQ-136 WHEN that pull request is merged, the daemon SHALL raise `merged` on the workspace; WHEN it is open and a check ended in failure, timeout or start-up failure, or a commit status in failure or error, it SHALL raise `ci_failed`.
- REQ-137 THE daemon SHALL raise an attention from a pull request only when what it sees differs from the last time (the pull request, merged, failing), ending the `merged` and `ci_failed` it had raised before, so that one a person has seen does not return while nothing changes; WHEN the branch has no pull request, it SHALL end them.
- REQ-138 IF `gh` is missing, fails or does not answer in time, THEN the daemon SHALL keep what it had and say it once in its log.
- REQ-139 WHEN the person presses `x` at the panel level of the terminal client on a pane whose shell is idle (no program running in it and no block running), THE client SHALL send `close_pane` for it at once.
- REQ-140 WHEN the person presses `x` on a pane where a program or a block is running, THE client SHALL ask first, naming it, and send `close_pane` only on `y`; any other key SHALL cancel.
- REQ-141 WHEN a pane is closed, THE workspace SHALL stay, even with no panes left, and the terminal client SHALL move its focus to a neighbouring pane when there is one.
- REQ-142 WHEN the person presses `x` at the workspace level, THE terminal client SHALL ask, and on `y` send `remove_workspace` without `remove_worktree`.
- REQ-143 WHERE the workspace is a worktree, THE question of closing a workspace SHALL also offer `d`, to remove the worktree as well.
- REQ-144 WHEN the person chooses `d`, THE terminal client SHALL read the workspace's `uncommitted` changes afresh (the daemon returns a node it still holds from an earlier look without reading it again); WHERE they list any file, it SHALL show those files (path and status), and go on as REQ-284 says (N71; before it the worktree could not be removed and only `c` closed the workspace).
- REQ-145 WHERE the `uncommitted` changes list no file, THE terminal client SHALL ask for `y` and then send `remove_workspace` with `remove_worktree`.
- REQ-146 IF the daemon answers a `remove_workspace` with an error (changes that appeared since the client looked, for one), THEN THE terminal client SHALL show the message and leave the workspace and its worktree as they are.
- REQ-147 WHEN a worktree is removed, THE daemon SHALL leave its branch in place.
- REQ-148 WHEN the person chooses «cerrar el panel» in the sheet of a long press on a pane's tab (REQ-182), THE phone SHALL open a sheet naming the pane and saying what closing does, and send `close_pane` only on «cerrar el panel»; it SHALL ask whatever runs in the pane.
- REQ-149 THE phone SHALL NOT offer to close a workspace or remove a worktree, and the sheet SHALL say that is done on the PC.
- REQ-150 IF the PC answers `close_pane` with an error or the link drops before it answers, THEN THE phone SHALL show the message and keep the pane's tab.
- REQ-151 THE terminal client SHALL read `mode`, `dark` and `light` from `settings.json`; WHERE the file is missing, unreadable, or holds a value it does not know or a theme of the other kind, it SHALL use `system`, `one-dark` and `one-light` for that value and not fail.
- REQ-152 WHEN the person presses `,` in the terminal client, it SHALL show settings with the mode and only the themes of the kind in force (the dark ones while the mode is dark, or `system` on a dark machine; the light ones otherwise); choosing one SHALL set the choice of that kind, apply it at once and write `settings.json` whole and atomically.
- REQ-153 WHILE the terminal client runs, it SHALL read `settings.json` again at most every 2 seconds and repaint when the mode or a theme changed.
- REQ-154 WHILE the mode is `system`, THE terminal client SHALL follow the machine's dark or light appearance (macOS `AppleInterfaceStyle`), and SHALL be dark where it cannot tell (no `defaults` to ask, or not macOS); a machine that answers that the key is not there is in light mode.
- REQ-155 THE terminal client SHALL draw every colour it chooses itself (backgrounds, borders, selection, marks, diff tints) from the theme in force.
- REQ-156 THE ten palettes SHALL be defined once in `contract/themes.json`, each saying whether it is `dark` or `light`, and each client SHALL have a test that its own table equals it.
- REQ-157 THE phone SHALL have an `ajustes` screen, opened from the gear of Home (REQ-216), with the mode and only the themes of the kind in force, keep a choice for each kind, apply it at once, keep them on the phone across launches, and send nothing to the PC for it.
- REQ-158 WHILE the phone's mode is `system`, THE phone SHALL follow Android's dark or light setting.
- REQ-159 WHEN the person presses `m` in the terminal client, it SHALL show the phone box and, WHERE the link is off, ask the daemon to turn it on from a thread of its own, so that the client keeps working while the tunnel opens.
- REQ-160 WHEN the link is on, THE box SHALL show the QR of the link (dark modules on a light ground with a quiet zone), the link, the local address, and the tunnel's address or that there is no tunnel; WHERE the area is too small for the QR, it SHALL show the link without it and say so.
- REQ-161 WHEN `esc` is pressed in the box, THE client SHALL close it and leave the link on; WHEN `s` is pressed it SHALL send `mobile_stop` and close it; WHEN `c` is pressed it SHALL copy the link.
- REQ-162 WHEN a phone asks to pair, THE client SHALL open the box with the question (the phone's name and the code), unless another question is open, in which case it SHALL wait for that one to be answered and say in the status bar that a phone is asking.
- REQ-163 WHILE the pairing question is shown, THE client SHALL answer yes only to `y` pressed at least 0.8 seconds after the question appeared, and no to any other key.
- REQ-164 WHEN the question is answered, THE box SHALL go back to the link, say whether the phone was paired or refused, and show how many phones are paired.
- REQ-165 IF the link cannot be turned on, THEN THE box SHALL show the daemon's reason and any key SHALL close it; WHEN `m` is pressed again it SHALL try again.
- REQ-166 WHILE the link is on, THE sidebar SHALL say `● teléfono · m`.
- REQ-167 THE phone's field to type a command SHALL rise with the keyboard, so that it stays above it.
- REQ-168 WHILE a pane's document, or the `blocks` node it names, has not arrived, THE clients SHALL draw grey bars where its lines will be, and SHALL NOT draw its screen, which would show for a moment and give way to the blocks.
- REQ-169 THE terminal client SHALL work at one of three levels, the workspaces of the sidebar, the panels of the open workspace, or typing in a panel; WHILE at each the status bar SHALL say WORKSPACE, PANEL or TERMINAL with the keys of that level, and the border of the area where the person is SHALL be blue (the sidebar's, or the focused panel's).
- REQ-170 WHEN `↵` is pressed on a workspace, or its row is clicked, THE client SHALL open it at the panel level, with the panel last focused; WHEN `↵` is pressed at the panel level, or a panel is clicked, it SHALL start typing in the focused panel.
- REQ-171 WHILE the client is at the terminal level, every key SHALL go to the program, `esc` and the `ctrl` keys included, except `^␣`, which SHALL take the client to the panel level.
- REQ-172 WHEN `esc` is pressed at the panel level, THE client SHALL go to the workspace level and show the panels as they were; at the workspace level `esc` SHALL do nothing.
- REQ-173 WHILE at the workspace level, `j` and `k` and the wheel over the sidebar SHALL move the highlight; WHILE at the panel level, `h`, `j`, `k`, `l`, the arrows and `tab` SHALL focus another panel (REQ-291).
- REQ-174 WHEN `x` is pressed, THE client SHALL close what the level is about: the workspace at the workspace level (REQ-142), the panel at the panel level (REQ-139).
- REQ-175 WHILE at the panel level, `n` SHALL ask the daemon for a new panel; `f` SHALL show the focused panel over the whole area, hiding the others, and a second `f` or `esc` SHALL show them again.
- REQ-176 WHEN `?` is pressed at the workspace or the panel level, THE client SHALL show a box with the keys of that level and any key SHALL close it; at the terminal level `?` SHALL go to the program.
- REQ-177 THE contract SHALL have the commands `rename_workspace {workspace, name}` and `rename_pane {pane, name}`, with fixtures and in the Kotlin types.
- REQ-178 WHEN `rename_workspace` arrives for a worktree, THE daemon SHALL give the worktree that name, trimmed, of at most 80 characters and with no control character, and keep it across a restart; an empty name SHALL bring back the one it had (`repo: branch`); a worktree's folder and branch SHALL NOT be touched; IF the workspace is a folder or the root of a repository (a directory on disk), THEN the daemon SHALL refuse with `bad_request` and change nothing; an unknown workspace SHALL be `not_found` and a name that is not allowed `bad_request`.
- REQ-179 WHEN `rename_pane` arrives, THE daemon SHALL give the pane that name as its title, which SHALL stay whatever program runs in it and after a restart; an empty name SHALL bring back what runs in it, else its shell; the same refusals as REQ-178.
- REQ-180 WHEN `r` is pressed at the workspace level of the terminal client on a worktree, it SHALL ask for its name (on a folder or a repository's root it SHALL do nothing), and at the panel level for that of the focused panel, with what is shown as the text to edit; `↵` SHALL send it, `esc` SHALL change nothing, and a `ctrl` chord SHALL be no letter of the name; at the terminal level `r` goes to the program.
- REQ-181 WHERE a worktree has the name it got when it was made (`repo: branch`), THE terminal client SHALL show its branch; WHERE it has another, it SHALL show that.
- REQ-182 WHEN a pane's tab is long-pressed, THE phone SHALL open a sheet with «nombrar el panel» and «cerrar el panel»; tapping outside the sheet or Back SHALL close it, and the tab SHALL carry no icon.
- REQ-183 WHEN a workspace row of Home, or the title of the workspace's screen, is long-pressed, THE phone SHALL open the sheet of that kind of workspace (REQ-227).
- REQ-184 WHEN «renombrar» or «nombrar» is chosen, THE phone SHALL show a field with the name as it is shown; «guardar» SHALL send `rename_workspace` or `rename_pane` with what is typed (empty: the automatic name); IF the PC refuses or the link drops, THEN the phone SHALL show the message and keep the sheet.
- REQ-185 WHERE a worktree has the name it got when it was made (`repo: branch`), THE phone SHALL show `↳` and its branch in the list, the header and the avisos; WHERE it has another, it SHALL show that name.
- REQ-186 WHEN `jwn` is run with no arguments, or as `jwn tui`, and no daemon answers on the socket, THE client SHALL start one in the background, with its output in `<state>/daemon.log`, wait up to 5 seconds for it, and say where the log is when it does not come up; WHEN one answers, it SHALL start nothing.
- REQ-187 WHEN the client ends, or the terminal it ran in closes, THE daemon it started SHALL keep running, with every pane; `jwn stop` SHALL end it.
- REQ-188 WHEN `mobile_start` arrives and the PC has a private address, THE daemon SHALL answer at once with the link over that address and the pairing token, bring the tunnel up in the background, and say `pending` until it is up or has failed; WHERE it has no private address, it SHALL wait for the tunnel (up to 90 seconds) as before.
- REQ-189 WHEN the tunnel comes up, THE daemon SHALL tell the connected phones its host (`endpoints`, REQ-82), and `mobile_link` SHALL answer with the link that includes it and the same pairing token, a new one only when that token is spent or has expired.
- REQ-190 THE daemon SHALL keep the tunnel it started until the link is turned off (`mobile_stop`, or the daemon ending), however many times `mobile_start` is asked, and whichever clients come and go.
- REQ-191 WHEN the terminal client's box shows a link whose tunnel is still opening, it SHALL say so and keep the link over the network working; WHEN the tunnel is up it SHALL show its address and a QR of the link that includes it, without losing a pairing question that was open.
- REQ-192 THE `create_worktree` command SHALL take an optional `folder` that names the worktree's folder inside `<repo>-wt/`, and SHALL refuse with `bad_request` one that is empty, `.`, `..`, over 80 characters, or has a `/`, a `\` or a control character.
- REQ-193 WHEN `o` is pressed at the workspace or the panel level of the terminal client, it SHALL open a folder browser that starts in the parent folder of the project of the highlighted workspace (home when there is none), lists that folder's visible subfolders sorted without regard to case, marks those already open and those that are git repositories, and shows the highlighted one's path, whether it is a repository and what it holds.
- REQ-194 WHILE the browser is open, typing SHALL filter its folders by letters in order; `↑` `↓` `ctrl-n` `ctrl-p` SHALL move; `→` or `tab` SHALL go into the highlighted folder; `←`, or `⌫` with nothing typed, SHALL go up; `~` with nothing typed SHALL go home; `ctrl-u` SHALL clear what was typed; `esc` SHALL close it.
- REQ-195 WHERE what was typed is a name a folder may have and no folder there has it, THE browser SHALL offer a row that makes the folder.
- REQ-196 WHEN `↵` is pressed on a row, THE client SHALL open that folder as a workspace with `open_folder` and give it a shell with `new_pane`, making the folder first when it is the row that makes one, and open the workspace at the panel level once the session shows it; WHERE the folder is already open, it SHALL go to that workspace and ask the daemon nothing.
- REQ-197 WHEN `w` is pressed at the workspace or the panel level, THE client SHALL open a box with a name (the first free `ws-N`, replaced by the first key typed) and what it will make: the branch `feat/<name>`, the branch it starts from (the highlighted workspace's) and the folder `<repo>-wt/<name>`; WHERE the highlighted workspace is not in a git repository it SHALL say so and open nothing.
- REQ-198 IF the name is not lowercase letters, digits and dashes, or the worktree is already open, or its folder is there, or its branch exists, THEN THE box SHALL say which instead of what it will make, and `↵` SHALL do nothing.
- REQ-199 WHEN `↵` is pressed with a name that can be used, THE client SHALL send `create_worktree` with the branch, `base` and the folder, give the workspace it makes a shell with `new_pane`, and open it at the panel level once the session shows it; IF the daemon refuses, THEN it SHALL show the message.
- REQ-200 WHILE the browser or the box is open, every key and click SHALL be theirs; at the terminal level `o` and `w` SHALL go to the program.
- REQ-201 WHEN the title or the legend of Home's workspace list is long-pressed, or its `+` is tapped, THE phone SHALL open a sheet «workspace nuevo» offering to open a folder of the PC, and SHALL NOT offer a worktree there.
- REQ-202 THE `list_dir` command SHALL reply with the canonical `path`, its `parent` (none at `/`), the daemon's `home`, the subfolders as `dirs` sorted without regard to case, each with whether it holds a `.git`, and their `total`.
- REQ-203 THE `list_dir` result SHALL hold at most 500 `dirs`, and `total` SHALL count all of the folder's subfolders.
- REQ-204 THE `list_dir` command SHALL leave out the subfolders whose names start with a dot unless `hidden` is true.
- REQ-205 IF the path of `list_dir` is relative, is not a folder, is missing or cannot be read, THEN THE daemon SHALL reply `bad_request`, `bad_request`, `not_found` or `io` respectively.
- REQ-206 THE workspace summaries of the `session` node SHALL carry `project` whenever the workspace node does.
- REQ-207 WHEN the phone's folder browser comes to a folder, it SHALL list its subfolders marking those with a workspace open in the session and those that are repositories, offer the crumbs from `~` (or `/`), `..` up and a toggle for hidden folders, and say how many of how many when the PC cut the list.
- REQ-208 WHEN the dock of the folder browser is pressed on a folder with no workspace, THE phone SHALL send `open_folder` and then `new_pane`, and open the workspace as soon as the session shows it; WHERE a workspace is already there, it SHALL open that one and send nothing.
- REQ-209 IF the PC refuses to open a folder or to give it a shell, THEN THE browser SHALL show the PC's message and stay where it is.
