Reads happen only while a client watches `uncommitted`, every 2 seconds, and a patch is sent only when the content changed; measured on this repository (N32): General of 203 files is a 1.1 MB snapshot read in 0.2 s, Sin commitear read in 0.09 s; a patch over the tunnel is not measured yet.
- RISK-24 `git diff HEAD` on a huge working tree takes noticeable time every 2 seconds while the node is open; the read runs without the hub lock, so other clients are not held, but the poll can lag behind the interval.
- RISK-25 Untracked files are read from disk by the daemon, not by git: a file being written while it is read can show a partial last line; the next read corrects it.
- RISK-26 The viewer's requests to the daemon (`open_diff` reads git) run in the terminal client's loop and wait up to 20 seconds, so a very large repository freezes the screen, and the live updates of the panes with it, while a view is first read. The node comes back in the next frame; a worker thread would remove the pause.
- RISK-27 `ChangesModel` and the screen decode a `diff` document each time they read it (the node cache keeps documents as JSON), up to three times per redraw for the tabs' counts; a 1 MB diff costs tens of milliseconds per redraw on the phone. A decoded copy kept per revision would remove it.
- RISK-30 `mergedAt` is not read (older `gh` prints a zero time for a pull request never merged); `state` decides. A pull request is looked at with one `gh` call a minute per worktree in use, a use of the person's GitHub quota; many worktrees open make many calls. What was last seen is not saved, so a restarted daemon raises `merged` or `ci_failed` again once for what it finds. A cancelled or skipped run is not a failure, and a check that is still running is not one yet, so a pull request whose only red check was cancelled shows nothing. The shape of `gh`'s JSON was checked against a real open and a real merged pull request of this repository; the commit-status shape (`state`) is from `gh`'s documentation.
- RISK-29 Only an agent the daemon started has hooks: a `claude` typed at a prompt has none and raises no attention (it also has no conversation id for a restart, RISK-12). Of the events, `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse` and `Stop` were seen firing with a real claude (2.1.299) through the daemon, and `agent_done` reached the pane; the `Notification` types and the tool names `AskUserQuestion` and `ExitPlanMode` are taken from Claude's documentation and were not triggered live. `PostToolUse` runs `jwn hook` after every tool, one process each.
- RISK-28 (closed by N39 and N41) The terminal client closes panes (`x`) and so does the phone (the `×` of its tab).
- RISK-31 A worktree seen clean may get changes before `remove_workspace` arrives; the daemon does not force, so git refuses and the client shows its message (REQ-146), which is the only guard. The sixteen named colours a command prints (palette indices 0 to 15 in a block's spans) are the theme's; black and bright white turn into the text colour on a light theme. The diff's tints are the ground with a little green or red in it (`look.rs`), not table entries, so a theme cannot get them wrong. The terminal client asks the machine for its appearance with one command (`defaults read -g AppleInterfaceStyle` on macOS); on Linux it cannot tell and stays dark, and a terminal that is light while the machine is dark is the person's to fix with the mode.
- RISK-32 The QR of the real link is large: with the tunnel and the private address the link is about 170 characters, a code of 57 modules, 33 terminal lines and 61 columns with its quiet zone, and the box needs more beside it. A terminal smaller than that shows the link without the QR (REQ-160), and the prototype's QR is only a drawing. Each `m` that finds the link off makes a new one-use token; the box of a link already on shows the token it had.
- RISK-33 (resolved by N52) The phone offered a worktree of any folder and showed git's refusal for one that is not a repository. `project` now tells which workspaces are repositories (REQ-222, REQ-225); a repository's `.git` is looked for on each rebuild of the documents (one stat per folder workspace), and a folder that gains or loses its `.git` while open changes group when the documents are next built.
- RISK-34 The keyboard's rules (REQ-236, REQ-237) have no test and a text field added without `keepsKeyboard()` loses its own focus on the tap that focuses it (the root takes the focus away at the end of every tap that nothing claimed). A tap in the middle of a drag-and-drop or a long press also counts as a tap that nothing claimed when it ends without moving.
- RISK-35 An entry's `name` labels the menu only: the pane's title is the foreground program the daemon finds by polling `ps` (RISK-11), so `command = ["sh", "-c", "…"]` shows as its first word and a program that ends at once leaves a plain shell. Carrying the name to the pane would add a field to `new_pane`. A `command` that does not exist is not caught: the shell says so and the pane stays a shell (RAT-7).
- RISK-36 The line of attention is a text of about 20 dp high inside a row 50 dp high, so its tap target is smaller than the 48 dp the platform recommends; a tap just beside it opens the workspace instead of the pane. Avisos, where the whole row opens the pane, is the way to be sure.
- RISK-37 The mode follows the command's first word as the shell reports it, so `cd x && claude`, `FOO=1 nvim` or `time less` are found only by the 0.7 s look (RISK-11) and show as a block for up to that long; and a command that the shell does not report (an integration that failed, REQ-55) never gets the mode from the command at all. The pane's `screen` and the node's `mode` still travel in separate messages, so a client may draw one frame of the pane's older screen on a switch; not seen, only inferred from the order of messages.
- RISK-38 `input` is read from the screen, so a right prompt (`RPROMPT`) drawn on the first row of the line shows in it as text after a gap, a buffer with hard line breaks is read from its last row only (its first row's prompt column does not apply), and a screen that was resized after the prompt ended keeps the old column. The text of a shell that never reports its prompt end is the screen's line, with the prompt in it (REQ-258). The field counts characters and not columns, so wide characters (CJK) wrap late. While a command runs what is typed goes to the command and the field cannot show it.
- RISK-39 Once claude has painted, its screen is the pane's whole screen, and claude draws below what was already there, so the shell's history shows above its interface; the loading only hides the screen while nothing is drawn. Starting with a blank screen would hide it, and is left for a later part if the history is in the way. A program whose first output is only blanks or escape sequences is `starting` until its first text. The phone gives up after 20 s on its own timer (its clock is not the PC's), the terminal by the block's start time.
- RISK-40 A program that leaves the alternate screen for a moment and stays (nvim `:!cmd`, a pager asked to run something) shows «cerrando <program>…» for up to 3 s, and a program that is quick to end (nvim) shows it for a frame or not at all; one that never uses the alternate screen (a program on the normal screen) is never «closing» and its old screen cannot be told apart from its own. The phone still shows the shell's screen in that second.
- RISK-41 A program that is not at a terminal that can take input (a shell at its prompt) receives an answer as typed text, as with a real terminal: `printf '\e[c'` at a prompt puts `^[[?62;22c` on the line, and `cat` of a file that holds queries answers them. The reply to `CSI > q` names the terminal as the product (`jwn(0.0.1)`) and a program that chooses features by that name does not know it. A mode is answered «not recognised» for every number, including those the screen tracks (bracketed paste, the mouse): a program that asks first and then does without loses those.
- RISK-42 The daemon still builds, serialises and compares every node of the tree at each refresh (at most once per 20 ms batch), so a pane of large blocks costs its size at each one, and about 180 MB of the daemon's memory is that pane's blocks held three times (the store, the view and the document). A guess at the front drop that is wrong gives a larger patch and never a wrong one; a list that loses more than 8 items at its front at once is patched item by item as before. The terminal client still holds each document twice (the client's JSON and the parsed node).
- RISK-43 A push may wait for something the daemon cannot give: a password, a passphrase, an unknown host. `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and the daemon having no terminal make git fail instead, but an SSH agent or a credential helper that asks through a window can wait; the client gives up after 120 seconds and says so, and the push may still finish. It is not guarded further.
- RISK-44 The tree has proportions, not sizes, so two terminal clients of different sizes draw the same arrangement at different cell counts, and which panel is on the left of another is the same for both but «the longer side» (`n`) is each client's own. A pane that shrinks under a few cells still draws a box with no room in it; the minimum is one cell of the split, not of what a shell needs.
- RISK-45 The blocks on disk hold whatever the commands printed, secrets included, for as long as the pane lives: the file is for the person alone (REQ-297) but is not encrypted, and a backup of the state folder takes it. Up to 4 MiB of JSON are serialised under the daemon's lock for each pane that changed, every 2 seconds at most, so a very noisy pane costs that much each time (not measured: `/perf-review` before trusting it with many noisy panes); `jwn stop` ends the daemon with a signal and the daemon does not save once more, so the output of a running command is kept up to the last 2 seconds (the command itself, and every command that started or ended, is kept at once); a block running at the crash is kept as it was at the last save.
- RISK-46 The selection is counted in lines from the first of the list: when the oldest blocks are dropped from the front (the 100-block limit) while it is lit, the same line numbers are other text, and it moves. A terminal that does not report the pointer moving with the button down (it needs the button-event mouse mode) gives no drag, only the click. The terminal's own selection (shift-drag in Ghostty) never reaches this client.
- RISK-47 The marks of a folder cost a `git status --ignored` limited to it, which in a very large working tree may take seconds, and its output is read up to 8 MiB; the tree does not watch the files, so what it shows is what was true when the folder was listed until `r` asks again; a search in a folder that is not a repository looks at up to 100 000 files and no deeper than eight folders.
- RISK-48 `setup` runs shell lines written in a file the repository carries, as the person: a repository whose `.jw.toml` was changed by someone else (a clone, a pull) runs their commands in the next worktree the person makes. The old jw did the same; here the box lists every line before `↵` (REQ-343), and so does the phone's sheet since N79 (REQ-349), which shows only the last folder of the `root`, a folder the person can write that may be anywhere; the phone runs nothing itself. `root`, the layout's commands and the agent's `start` are the file's too.
- RISK-49 A command that writes to a pane writes to whatever is there: `prompt --pane` aimed at a plain shell runs the text as a command, and a text that reaches an agent that is waiting for an answer to a question or a permission answers it. The agent is recognised by the program's name (`claude`, `codex`), so one started through a wrapper (`npx`, a script) is not found until `--pane` names it; `codex` has no hooks and raises no attention. «Quiet for a second» can be a model thinking without moving the screen, and the text then reaches an agent that was not asking; and a pane that animates for 40 seconds makes `prompt` fail where `--now` would have written. The pane's id in the environment is trusted: an agent can only be kept from writing to itself because it says who it is.
- RISK-50 What the engine does for these is *docs* or *unverified*, not tested (`docs/claude-engine.md`): stopping a task while it runs (`stop_task`), the choice Claude asks for and the approval of a plan arriving as `can_use_tool`, the rule it takes back for «for this session» (the daemon keeps its own), images as content blocks and `@` in the stream, the checklist's tools (`TaskCreate`; `TodoWrite` is off by default), `ScheduleWakeup` and cron, the page tool, `PushNotification` and workflows. The first task of each part is to try its own with a real Claude, record it in `docs/claude-engine.md` and only then build on it; where it fails the fallback is in the REQ (an error that says so for stopping a task; the text of the question as a normal message; the checklist and the page absent from the node) and the part says so in Corrections. Tested for N81 with the real engine: the turn, the permission, the interrupt, `set_permission_mode`, `/effort`, the resume.
- RISK-51 Claude Code changes what it says between versions (a tool called `Task` became `Agent`): the daemon ignores a message it does not know and keeps going, takes the version from `init`, and `docs/claude-engine.md` says which version each fact was run on. A test runs a fake engine that speaks the recorded messages, and a manual check against the installed `claude` is part of each part.
- RISK-52 The person's own `~/.claude` runs in the engine: its SessionStart hooks print (4 of them in the author's runs), its MCP servers may need a login, and its settings may allow or forbid tools. The daemon hides what is not the conversation and does not try to log anything in.
- RISK-53 `!command` runs as the person in the pane's folder with no terminal: a program that waits for a key hangs until the 60 seconds are over and is killed; one that needs a terminal (an editor) does not work and the client says so. The pid is the daemon's own and nothing else is ever signalled.
- RISK-54 A shell function `claude` of the person's, an alias or a `PATH` entry of theirs may already stand where the integration's goes. The integration defines its function last, after the person's rc, and only when none of theirs is a function; one that is an alias keeps winning and the conversation is not offered. A shell that is not zsh, bash or fish never gets one.
- RISK-55 `auto` and `accept edits` let Claude edit and run without asking, and the phone can change the mode from a pocket. The mode is shown on the pill and in the footer in a colour of its own, a change is a note in the conversation and `bypassPermissions` and `dontAsk` are refused (REQ-390); whether the phone should ask before it leaves `manual` is open (OPEN-11).
- RISK-56 A turn costs what it costs in Claude's own screen and a conversation that streams large outputs makes large patches: a patch of a text that grows replaces the whole string, so at 20 a second a 48 KB answer sent 2.7 MB to a client (56 times the text) and kept its last 256 patches in memory (10 MB for the daemon; 48 MB for 180 KB); REQ-378 spaces the patches by the length of the text, which sent 0.5 MB and 8 MB, 2.6 MB and 9 MB. Measured 2026-10-16 with a release build and a fake engine (`docs/claude-engine.md` has the real one's costs): CPU while it streams is 1.3 % to 1.4 % of a core, idle 0.1 % with a conversation open (3.5 MB and 0.3 % for `jwn claude`'s plain view), 25 other panes add 0.8 %, a node of 300 items is 13 KB and its daemon 8.9 MB and does not grow past that, and saving a turn costs 4.7 ms of CPU with or without 300 items. The daemon bounds what it keeps (300 items, 40 lines of a tool's output, 2 MiB saved).
- RISK-57 A conversation started in Claude's own screen is not served by the daemon, and resuming one of those headless was not tested: `/resume` lists what is on disk and the first message after it says if the engine could not read it; since N100 what it said is read from the transcript and shown (REQ-540), but resuming one of those with the real engine was tried only with a stand-in.
- RISK-58 `/doctor` and `/insights` ran as long model calls in the tests; they are marked as needing Claude's screen so a person does not start one without knowing.
- RISK-59 `list_worktrees` waits for the `git status` of every worktree and `git::read` has no time limit: a worktree on a network folder or with a lock that stays can hold the answer for ever, and the terminal client, which waits 20 s for it, then shows nothing in the box. Reading the status of a worktree with a limit, or leaving `changes` out when it takes long, is not built.
- RISK-60 A conversation made from its transcript depends on Claude keeping it: Claude Code deletes old transcripts after a time it is set to (`cleanupPeriodDays`), and then the chat of that conversation is gone from jwn too, as it is from `claude --resume`; what jwn kept (its notes and costs) stays, with no chat around it. A copy of the chat would avoid it and goes stale (RAT-52); the user chose the transcript.
- RISK-61 A session that jwn only follows shows no working line (the time and the tokens of the turn that runs, which Claude Code shows with its spinner): the transcript says a turn began and says when it ended, but not how many tokens it has used, and a turn that ended by an interruption says nothing. The tools of a turn are listed one by one, where Claude Code folds them («Ran 2 shell commands»); a prototype of how jwn folds them is not made. (N107b: it does show it now, REQ-565, with a turn that is open for at most 15 minutes of silence.)
- RISK-62 A message sent from a conversation that follows a turn of another `claude` starts jwn's engine with `--resume` on a session that the other one is still writing, and the two write the same transcript. This was so before N107b, and the node says it now (`following`); nothing warns or queues. A warning or a wait for the turn to end is a choice for the person, not built.
- RISK-63 Two lines that each make an item and have the same millisecond would lose the one that is older at a boundary between pages: items are asked for by the time of the first one held. None were found in 1,217 item lines of a 437 MB transcript. Older pages hold the items of the transcript and those of jwn's own that its memory still has (`kept()` forgets the ones that leave its window, which was so before N108), and the card of a subagent is the tool that began it.
- RISK-4 The Kotlin conformance suite proves the types and the patch rules, not a live connection; the live flows of the phone are checked by hand against a real daemon (RISK-10).

## Open questions
- OPEN-12 Whether the daemon answers a program's question about the background (OSC 11, and 10 and 4) with the theme of the client that looks, so nvim picks its light variant on a light theme: not built (the user, 2026-10-16, chose colours only); it contradicts RAT-25 and has no answer when two clients have two themes. `docs/prototypes/screen-colours.html` shows both.
- OPEN-11 Whether the phone asks before it leaves `manual` for `accept edits`, `auto` or `plan` (RISK-55), and whether `codex` gets the same conversation later (it has no JSON mode that was looked at; until then it stays in a pane's own screen).
- OPEN-10 Several sessions in one daemon (RAT-16): not built; reconsider when a person routinely has more than about 30 workspaces, or needs to separate contexts that share one attention list.
- OPEN-9 The viewer of files and Markdown (the old jw's reader): opening a file from a diff, and Markdown rendered in both clients. The user (2026-10-16) asked for the Markdown viewer first, because he wants Claude's plan shown in it: N88 to N90. Still open: a viewer of other text, opening a `.md` from a diff, following links between documents, and an editor.
- OPEN-8 Whether a phone may be read-only (one switch, or per device) and whether a device secret should expire.
- OPEN-1 (closed) Retention: how many revisions per node the daemon keeps for resume; it is 256 (`RETAIN` in `crates/daemon/src/core.rs`).
- OPEN-3 (closed) The permission and question screens inside the claude pane: they are the conversation's box and sheet (REQ-404, REQ-441).
- OPEN-4 (closed) The terminal client's workspace list has its prototype, `docs/prototypes/tui.html` (N11).
- OPEN-6 Mirroring the shell's line buffer into the clients' editors (what you type at a terminal shows in the phone's editor) and the other way round.
- OPEN-7 History and completion in the editors: from the shell, or kept by the client.
- OPEN-5 Where the special keys (`esc`, `tab`, arrows, `^C` beyond the stop button, `^D`, `^R`, …) go on the phone now that the key row is removed (the user: think about it later; it is among the last things to do).
- OPEN-2 (closed) Whether a `pane` screen needs styled cells: it stayed text and styled output lived in the `blocks` node (RAT-8); N98 gave the alternate screen its colours (RAT-49).

## Corrections
- A client that resumed with the connection's epoch instead of its cached document's epoch would keep a dead daemon's data (found by test in N2); the client now uses the document's.
- The schema's `$id` was the relative `contract.schema.json`; the Rust validator accepted it and the Kotlin one refused it (not an absolute URI). It is now `https://schema.jwn.invalid/contract.schema.json`.
- Gradle called the Kotlin test task up to date when only a fixture changed, so drift would have passed silently; `contract/` is now declared a task input (found by mutating a fixture).
- Patch application had only Rust unit tests; it now has shared `fixtures/patch-cases/` that both languages apply (REQ-31).
- Running the live app on a phone found three bugs no test had: a socket write on the UI thread crashed it (`NetworkOnMainThreadException`; writes now go through their own thread); Compose's strong skipping meant a node arriving did not redraw the screen (the screens now read the source's tick themselves); and a daemon started inside a terminal window dies with the window (start it detached).
- Settle who clears an attention: the task proposed a per-client *seen* and the first build did that (`e2e-live.py` even tested it). The user then asked that clearing on the phone show on the terminal and the other way round, so it is shared through `mark_seen` (REQ-46, REQ-47).
- The QA bugs B-28, B-29 and B-30 (an exited agent looking alive, a restart failing with "Session ID … already in use", two Claudes sharing one transcript) are closed by design here: the pane is always a shell, a restart uses `--resume` with an id given at launch, and every agent has its own id (REQ-50 to REQ-52, tested with a stand-in `claude` that saves a transcript like the real one; the real one was checked to start with the injected id and be detected).
- The task note said a binary kind and a PROTOCOL integer were needed for the phone; they are not part of this contract.
- REQ-37 cited `docs/specs/mobile-prototype.html`, the old jw's prototype, and said the key row changes; the design here is `docs/prototypes/mobile.html` and the key row is gone (REQ-38, withdrawn).
- RISK-4 said no Kotlin client had talked to the daemon; the phone has since (N9, N27, N34). OPEN-1, OPEN-2 and OPEN-4 stayed listed as open after they were decided.
- The fish integration had never been run (RISK-16); its first run in N35 found the shared test written in bash syntax and fish 4's own OSC 133 marks arriving before ours, which the scanner now tolerates.
- REQ-54, 57, 59 and 60 each held several claims, and REQ-60 was not in EARS form; each now holds one and the rest are REQ-122 to REQ-127 (ids are never renumbered).
- N38 described five themes, each with a dark and a light member, and one `theme` setting. The user asked that each palette say whether it is dark or light and that a selector list only the kind in force; the file is now ten palettes with a `kind`, and the settings hold a theme for each kind (REQ-151, 152, 156, 157).
- Until N43 `↵` on a workspace started typing in its panel at once, `x` and `X` closed a panel and a workspace, and `esc` at the sidebar quit; the user proposed levels (workspaces, panels, terminal) and approved the prototype `docs/prototypes/tui-levels.html`: `↵` now goes in a level, `x` closes what the level is about, `esc` goes up and only `q` quits.
- REQ-80 said the QR was shown only after the tunnel resolved and the tunnel ended with `jwn pair`; the first made a person wait up to a minute and a half for a link that already worked over the network, and the second is not what the user wants: the network link comes first, the tunnel is told to the phone when it is up, and it stays until the link is turned off (REQ-188 to REQ-190).
- The plan for part 4 listed sessions and settings as parity with the old jw; the user cut them: no sessions (RAT-16) and settings only for the look (mode and theme), without the old leader key, animations or themes of their own. `remove_worktree` stays unforced.
- The N50 prototype showed a disabled «worktree nuevo» on a folder that is not a repository and `● abierta`/`git` marks on the dock; the phone cannot know the first (RISK-33) and `list_dir` does not say whether the folder itself is a repository, so the sheet offers it and shows the PC's refusal, and the dock names only the path.
- REQ-113 and REQ-157 said the phone had a `diff` tab and an `ajustes` tab in a bottom bar of four (workspaces, avisos, diff, ajustes). N51 took the bar away: the diff is part of a workspace, and avisos and ajustes are opened from the header of Home. The search of the prototype's header is not built: the app has no finder yet.
- Until N52 the terminal's sidebar and the phone's Home listed the workspaces flat, in the order they were opened, with only `↳` to say a worktree was one; the old jw's grouping (root, worktrees in, folders apart) came back at the user's request. N50's RAT-19 and RISK-33 said the phone could not tell which workspaces are repositories; `project` on a repository's root now says it.
- N52's phone list had the new order but not the look of the prototype (the mark at the left, `↳` inside the name, a borderless «ir ›»), and the terminal drew `loom` twice, with its repository's heading between, when the client was new and the daemon not yet restarted (an older daemon does not say a folder is a root). The row was redrawn as the prototype and REQ-226 makes the clients understand a root by its path.
- N45 and N41 said the phone only closes a pane and that closing a workspace or removing a worktree is the PC's (the RAT-less decision of 2026-10-14); the user asked in N54 for the buttons on the phone, with a sheet for each kind of workspace. REQ-183 and REQ-213 changed, REQ-225 is withdrawn, and the sentence about the PC is gone from the sheet that closes a pane (REQ-148).
- The N51 correction said the app has no finder and the prototype's search was not built; N55 builds it. The prototype's sheet said «por último uso» but listed in the order of Home; it now orders by the workspaces opened, as the phone does.
- REQ-154 and the code treated a failure of `defaults read -g AppleInterfaceStyle` as «cannot tell» and so as dark; macOS fails exactly when the machine is in light mode, so the terminal client in `system` mode was dark on every machine and never followed it. Found when the user said it did not change with the system (N59): a failure is now light, and only not being able to ask is dark.
- REQ-34 and REQ-224 (N53) said a Home row has a bordered «ir ›» under its attention; the user found it pointless on the workspace's row, and N61 removes it: the attention is a line of coloured text in the row (REQ-247, REQ-248), and Avisos loses it too.
- REQ-58 had the node in mode `full` while a known program was in the foreground, and the foreground was only looked at every 0.7 s, so on every entry to claude its screen showed first as a block, and on every exit the shell's raw screen showed for up to 0.7 s after the command had ended (reproduced in N62 on a private daemon). The mode now follows the shell's own report of the command (REQ-250, REQ-251) and the look at the foreground is the fallback.
- RAT-11 said the line is the shell's and a client sends the finished line, and the terminal's field was a copy of the screen's cursor row with the shell's prompt in it; N63 draws the field from `input` (the line without the prompt) while the shell still owns the line.
- N62 made the node `full` the moment a command that takes the screen is reported, and the first thing it showed was the pane's screen, which for a program that draws on the normal screen is still the shell's as it was until the program paints (a second, longer «terminal original» than the 0.7 s it fixed); N64 draws that wait as a loading.
- N64 hid the shell's old screen at the start of a program but not at its end: the node stays `full` until the shell says the command ended, and a program that leaves the alternate screen first and ends a second later (claude) left the shell's screen showing for that second; N65 draws it as closing.
- N65 and N62 treated the second a program takes to start or to end as something to draw; for neovim it was a wait that the terminal itself caused, by never answering `CSI c`, and nothing was on the screen to detect (it quits with its screen still up). The wait is gone (N66, RAT-25); the loadings stay for what remains (claude's own shutdown).
- N44 and N45 let a workspace of any kind be renamed, a folder and a repository's root included. A folder is a directory on disk, and its shown name is its directory's, so naming it only made two names for one thing; the user dropped it on 2026-10-15 and N68 keeps the rename for worktrees and panes (REQ-178, REQ-180, REQ-227). A folder named before N68 keeps the name it was given until the session is made again.
- N38 and N39 said a worktree with changes could not be removed and that `remove_worktree` stays unforced (REQ-21, REQ-144). The user decided on 2026-10-15 that it may be, by force, after the branch's name is typed (N71, RAT-28); the branch still stays, and the phone still never forces.
- The terminal client arranged the panels by itself, in the order they were made: one, two side by side, and from three the first on the left with the rest stacked (N43). From N72 that arrangement is the daemon's default tree (REQ-288) and `j` and `k` no longer go to the next and the previous panel but below and above; `tab` is the next.
- Row 9 of what was left said that a restarted daemon gave new shells with an empty screen. The shells are new and the screen is empty, but since N74 the blocks of each pane come back before them (a program that had the whole screen does not).
- N74 saved a pane's blocks at most every 2 seconds and once more when the daemon was stopped from inside, and `jwn stop` ends the daemon with a signal that never reaches that last save: a command started less than 2 seconds after the previous write was not on disk and, after the restart, was missing instead of `interrumpido`. Found by the user («no se ve la línea…») and then by a run with the installed binary; the blocks are now written at once whenever their shape changes (REQ-297).
- N49 said that the terminal client's `w` sends `feat/<name>` as the branch and that the worktree's folder is `<repo>-wt/<name>`; from N78 the branch's template and `root` come from the repository's `.jw.toml` and those two are only the defaults (REQ-198 and REQ-199 are checked on what the box shows, REQ-344).
- REQ-210 and REQ-212 (N50) said the phone's sheet shows `feat/<name>` and `<repo>-wt/<name>` and opens the worktree with `new_pane`; from N79 the branch, the folder and the panes come from the repository's `.jw.toml` and the sheet asks `start_workspace`, so a worktree made on the phone gets the setup and the layout the terminal client gives it.
- The open question about the permission and question screens inside the claude pane still offering their options (OPEN-3) is closed by the conversation: for a `claude` typed in a pane they are the box of the terminal client and the sheet of the phone (REQ-404, REQ-441); what the pane's own screen shows is Claude's, as before, for `command claude`.
- The *Agent attention* paragraph said that an agent the daemon starts gets hooks. It still does for a `claude` started without the integration's function (`command claude`, or a shell that is not zsh, bash or fish); for a conversation the engine's own events raise the attention and no hook is added (REQ-374).
- Designing the conversation assumed three things the engine's output showed false (2026-10-15): that `/cost` prints the conversation's cost (it prints the subscription's use: REQ-419), that `!command` is a shell mode of the engine (it is sent to the model as text: REQ-394) and that the screens `/tasks`, `/artifacts`, `/resume` and the like exist in it (they answer «isn't available in this environment»: REQ-424).
- The first design changed the model and the effort with control messages; `/model` and `/effort` typed as a line print and set them in the engine (tested for the print form, *docs* for the set form), which is simpler, so a control message is only the fallback for the mode (REQ-389).
- N81 built REQ-375 as «a new conversation starts its engine when it opens; a resumed one, at the next message» (the first text said the engine starts at every open, and REQ-393 said a restored conversation waits for a message: both cannot hold). REQ-384 raised `question` and `plan` from the start; they are raised with the questions that make them (REQ-435, REQ-436, N84), so N81 raises `permission` only.
- The first design had `open_conversation` carry the path of the engine; it carries the environment `claude` was typed in (`env`) and the daemon finds the program on that `PATH`, so no path of a program travels on the wire and the engine runs with what the person's shell had. The answer to a question gained `by`, so each client says where it came from (REQ-405).
- The pane's environment now carries `JWN_SOCKET` and `JWN_STATE_DIR` of its daemon as well as `JWN_BIN` and `JWN_CONVERSATION`: before, only `JWN_PANE_ID` travelled and what ran in a pane reached the default daemon by luck, which for a test daemon was the person's own. The swap was off until `JWN_CONVERSATION=1` was set for the daemon; since N82 it is on, and `JWN_CONVERSATION=0` turns it off (the shell function checks it each time it is called) (2026-10-16).
- REQ-400 asked for «the tokens written» on the working line; the node has no count of them (the engine reports them only when a turn ends), so the line says what Claude does, for how long and `esc interrumpe` (2026-10-16).
- REQ-404's third option says «decirle a Claude qué hacer distinto»; the terminal client's `n` (or `3`) refuses and what to do instead is the next message, which is what Claude's own screen asks of `esc`. `conversation_answer` still takes `text` for a client that asks for it (2026-10-16).
- Choosing an item (REQ-410): while one is picked `c`, `t`, `esc` and `↑` `↓` act on it and any other key lets it go and is typed in the line; a click on a tool, a subagent or a `!` line also opens or closes it (REQ-399). The terminal asks for the bracketed paste, so a paste arrives as one piece: into the conversation's line as it is, and into a shell or a box of the client as the keys it always was, a line break being Enter (2026-10-16).
- REQ-408: the files for an `@` are asked for at each new fragment, from the second character that follows it on, and an answer for what is no longer typed is dropped (2026-10-16).
- `/plan` was missing from the list (2026-10-16): the engine run headless does not list it, because there plan is a permission mode and not a command, and jwn offered it only as `/mode plan` and `⇧tab`. It is one of jwn's own now (REQ-418). Neither were the rest of Claude's: the person asked for all of them (REQ-460), so the commands the engine does not serve (`/help`, `/permissions`, `/rewind`…) are in the list as `screen`, from the commands page of the documentation, and N83b turns the eight that jwn draws itself into `panel`. A conversation restored from a file written before a command existed gets it, since its engine (and so its list) comes only with the next message.
- REQ-425 promised the engine's version in `/status`, the rules of the session in `/permissions` and the lists on the phone through the same code; none of those is in the node, so `/status` shows what the node holds (model, effort, mode, session, folder, cost and limits), `/permissions` shows the settings files' rules (the user's, the project's and the local ones) without the session's, and the phone draws its own lists in N85 (2026-10-16).
- `/continue` and `/resume` were not offered at all, and the person found it («no muestra la pantalla de resumen»): they were listed as screen commands until the list existed. `/continue` is `/resume`'s other name in Claude and here takes the newest conversation at once (2026-10-16).
- A line «Modo: plan» was added to the conversation each time the mode changed (N81); the person asked why it was on the screen, since the footer already says the mode in its colour, so it is gone (2026-10-16).
- N84 tried each message with the real `claude` first (RISK-50, 2026-10-16; `docs/claude-engine.md`): `AskUserQuestion` and `ExitPlanMode` arrive as `can_use_tool` and are answered with `answers` and with allow or deny; `stop_task` works with a background `Bash`; a subagent's own permission requests arrive as ordinary ones, with its `agent_id`. What the first design assumed and was false: the headless engine of this version has **no checklist tool** (`TodoWrite`, `TaskCreate` are absent, and the environment variables that would add them add nothing) and **no `Artifact` tool**, so REQ-434 is built for `TodoWrite`'s documented shape without having seen it, and REQ-437 (pages) is not built; `todos` and `artifacts` stay empty with this engine. The subagent's tool is called `Task` here and `Agent` in other versions (both are handled).
- A question's answer key is the number of the option (`0`, `1`…), and the numbers with commas for a choice of several; the second question of a call has its own ask id (`<request>:<n>`) so that a late answer for the first is refused. The engine also takes any text as the person's own answer (its «Other»); the clients do not offer it yet. A plan has three answers: `accept` (the engine is asked for `acceptEdits` at once, in the same turn), `manual` and `no` (the text goes with it). A subagent's permission request is shown like any other.
- A subagent or a task that ran in front is cut when its turn ends, and every one of them when the engine is gone, so that no card or task stays `run` for ever and the footer's `⚙ n` does not count what died (REQ-428, REQ-429; the review of N84, 2026-10-16).
- Scheduled loops (`CronCreate`, `ScheduleWakeup`) and `Workflow` are listed in this engine but were not tried, so no task of kind `loop` is made.
- REQ-448 put the tasks pill in the header; the header is shared with the shell panes, so the pill sits in the bar under the tab, with the checklist, and is shown only for a conversation. The `■` that stops a task is drawn on the tasks sheet since N84 (2026-10-16).
- REQ-445's `Foto` opens the system camera app (the app already holds the camera permission for the QR) and `Galería` the system picker, so neither needs a new permission; the photo is written under the cache folder through a `FileProvider` and made small like any picture. `/permissions` and `/memory` read files of the PC and are not drawn on the phone: it says so and points at the terminal (REQ-450, 2026-10-16).
- The phone names the tab of a pane that runs `claude` as `claude · <name>` with the conversation's colour under it (the open tab only, since only its node is open), and a tap on that tab opens the settings (REQ-444).
- N83 was split in two (2026-10-16): the person found that `/` opened nothing and that the footer said `claude` for the model, which are the list and the model at the start; the screens of jwn that the list also names (`/help`, `/status`, `/resume`…) are N83b, and until then they are not offered (the engine answers «isn't available» to them). The model was `claude` until a first turn's `init` said it, because the engine does not announce it before; `initialize` does, with every model it has.
- A conversation whose pane's foreground `claude` was seen and is gone (killed, crashed) is ended by the daemon; one that was never seen in the foreground (opened by a client directly) is not, so a conversation cannot be ended by a look that happened before the program started.
- N81's first build restarted the engine (`--resume` with a new `--permission-mode`) to change the mode, and a run against the real `claude` showed what that costs: a new process pays the cache of a conversation again (about 0.1 USD with the author's plugins). The engine takes `set_permission_mode` while it runs and says when it did, so the mode is asked of the engine that runs and nothing restarts (REQ-389). The same run found that a conversation resumed after the daemon restarted came back with the default model, since only the short name was saved: what `--model` and `--effort` asked is saved with it now, and what a local command (`/effort`) answers is an `out` item, not a message of Claude's.
- REQ-378 first said «at most every 50 ms» and a measurement (2026-10-16) showed what that costs when the answer is long: each patch carries the whole text so far (the patches only replace a string), so a 48 KB answer sent a client 2.7 MB and the 256 patches the daemon keeps for resuming weighed 10 MB of its memory, against 0.5 MB and 8 MB once the pace follows the length of the text. The cost is that the end of a long answer shows twice a second and not twenty times; a text shorter than 4 KiB is shown as before.
- REQ-397 to REQ-415 drew a row for each tool; since N105 the tools that have ended and are of a class that folds are one row for each run (REQ-558, RAT-55), and open as they were drawn.
- The prototype drew the tool that runs as `● Ejecutando cargo test · N s`, `⎿ $ cmd` and `ctrl+b para dejarlo en segundo plano`; the clients draw `● Bash(cargo test) · 8 s` as every tool is, `⎿ ejecutando…`, and no `ctrl+b`, because jwn's engine has no control to put a tool in the background.
- REQ-400 said that the tokens written were left out because the node had no count; since N107 it has one for the conversations jwn drives (REQ-563). A session that is only followed still has no working line (RISK-61): the transcript does give a count for each message, so the prototype's reason to leave tokens out there was wrong, and the line, the tool that runs there and the tokens are N107b. The «sin resultado» of a tool of a turn that is open is no more: it runs (REQ-566).
- The prototype of N108 said how many items there are before the first one («▲ 940 anteriores»). `first` is not a total (it is where the window is in what was read, and a window read from a later place in the file says a smaller number), so the row says that there are more and not how many. The prototype's «con un toque» way of loading is not built: the list loads when it reaches its top, and a click on the row asks again after a failure.
- REQ-398 drew Claude's text in cyan code and REQ-440 drew the phone's text flat; since N106 a finished text with Markdown is drawn as blocks (REQ-560, REQ-561), and one with none as before. The prototype showed a link's address dimmed after its text; the viewer's drawing has none and the conversation does not add it, so the address is only in `blocks` for now. The mark «· resumen» of a summary of thinking is N107's.
- The reader of N100 and N101 took a `thinking` block, an `attachment` and a `system` line for lines of no conversation, and so dropped Claude's narration between its tools, the messages typed while it worked and the end of its turns; REQ-550 to REQ-552 read them (RAT-54).
- The chip «en cola N» was all that showed of the messages typed while a turn ran, in both clients; the messages themselves are drawn since N103 (REQ-549), and a session followed from a transcript shows what the other `claude` has queued.
- REQ-372 listed `--resume [id]` among what the engine serves, but the daemon refused a `--resume` with no id, so `claude --resume` ran Claude's own screen; the binary now makes the choice (REQ-547, RAT-53).
- REQ-393 said a conversation that comes back has the items jwn saved; since N101 the chat is made from the engine's transcript when it is there and jwn saves only what is its own (REQ-544), while a conversation saved before keeps its items.
- REQ-375 said a resumed conversation starts its engine when the next message comes and nothing about what is shown meanwhile; N81 to N99 showed an empty conversation with the `resume` divider when jwn had nothing saved for the pane, at the default mode, model and effort. REQ-540 to REQ-543 read the transcript (RAT-51).
- The prototype of the worktrees that exist (N99) changed the side of the box with `tab` and said that going into an opened worktree starts its panel as the settings say; `tab` already chooses between a shell and `claude` in the box (REQ-510), so the sides are `←` and `→`, an opened worktree has no panel until the person goes in, and the count `existentes N` is there only once the side has been looked at (RAT-50).
- OPEN-2 and RAT-8 said a pane's screen stays text and styled output lives in `blocks`; since N98 a screen on the alternate screen carries its colours too (RAT-49), because the programs that take the whole screen are the ones whose output the blocks do not hold.
- N97 (2026-10-16, the user, building the project screen): the prototype's `e` (open the file in the editor) was not built: since N95 there is no editor panel, and a person who wants the file opens it from a panel. The screen is for repositories; a folder that is no repository has none yet.
- N96 (2026-10-16, the user): the branch of a new worktree is the name the person types, as written, not the settings' template: REQ-512 to REQ-515 replace the template of REQ-330 (`branch` `feat/{name}`), the `branch` of REQ-331 and REQ-334's reply, the marker of `branch` in REQ-333, and the «branch (the template with `{name}` replaced)» of REQ-343, REQ-344 and REQ-349; the name `ws-1` that the box suggests is now the branch `ws-1`, no longer `feat/ws-1`. The project screen is N97.
- N95 (2026-10-16, the user): a workspace opens with **one** panel, a shell or `claude`, which the settings' `open` and the person's choice say; the layout is gone. REQ-337, REQ-338's «leaf», REQ-339 to REQ-341 (a `setup` pane below a layout), REQ-346 (the design-studio layout), REQ-332's `ratio`, the layout and pane count of REQ-333, the «programs of the panes» of REQ-343 and REQ-349, and the layout of REQ-500 are replaced by REQ-504 to REQ-511: the setup runs in the one panel, then the shell stays or `claude` starts. The panes can still be split by hand (N72).
- REQ-336 to REQ-341 said a folder gets one shell whatever its repository's file says; since N94 (REQ-500) a folder has the layout of its settings, and only the layout.
- RAT-34 and REQ-330 called `.jw.toml` «the settings of a repository»; since N91 they are a file of the repository *or* a personal one of the same syntax (REQ-482), the repository's first. The box and the sheet no longer say «sin .jw.toml» but «sin configuración: como siempre».
- REQ-325 said that a file of the phone's list does nothing when tapped; REQ-479 changes it for a `.md` (it opens in the viewer) and any other file now says that the phone does not open it. A link still does nothing. The phone shows code without the terminal's tones (a block with its language), and links are underlined but not followed.
- The viewer replaces the list of the plan's lines that `v` opened over the question (N84): that list could show only the 200 lines of 300 characters the node keeps, and could not be searched, copied or answered from. REQ-475 answers from the viewer with the box's own keys; REQ-469 keeps the first lines in the box.
- N87 copies the text as drawn, not Claude's source: the `**` and backticks of Markdown are not in it (they are not on the screen); `c` on a picked item is still the way to copy an item's own text (REQ-410). A selection is counted in rows of the list from its first, so when the width changes (a resize) or the oldest items are dropped from the node while it is lit, the same numbers are other text, as RISK-46 says of a pane's list.
- REQ-454 said the exit is 3 when the turn *ends* with a question; a turn with a question never ends until it is answered, so `prompt --wait` stops the moment a question or a permission waits (2026-10-16). A message sent while one already waits is queued by the daemon and does not answer it, so `prompt` to a conversation is safe where a paste into Claude's own screen was not; the skill says so.
- REQ-455 changes what `read` prints for a pane with a conversation: the items, not the pane's terminal, so the plain version `claude` prints for a client that does not draw it (the banner, the prompt) is not in it. `--blocks` is unchanged. The tests that looked at a pane's screen through `read` read the pane node's screen instead.
- `worktree --task` waits up to 10 s (less when `JWN_AGENT_WAIT_MS` is shorter) for the new agent's conversation when the agent is `claude`, and types the task into the pane as before when none opens (an agent started without the swap).

## Tests
`cargo test -p contract` (`crates/contract/tests/conformance.rs`): fixtures round-trip and validate, invalid fixtures are rejected, every schema variant has a fixture, unknown fields are ignored, Rust enums match the schema. Mutating a fixture or a Rust field turns it red.

### What enforces each REQ
REQ-139 to REQ-147 by N39: `crates/jwn/src/tui/manage.rs` tests (what each key does in each question) and `tui.rs` tests (idle pane closes at once, one that runs asks and names it, a workspace not opened gives no pane to close, the focus clamps, `X` never asks to remove the worktree, `d` on a folder does nothing, a worktree with changes lists them and only `c` goes on, a clean one is removed on `y` and `esc` goes back and lets go of the node, a refusal is shown, and keys and clicks do not reach the panes under a question); `crates/daemon/tests/panes.rs` (closing the last pane leaves the workspace, REQ-141) and `workspaces.rs` (the branch stays after the worktree is removed, REQ-147). Driven through a pty against a real daemon and real git: `x` on an idle pane, `X`, `d` with two changed files (listed, `y` ignored, `c` closed only the workspace and the folder stayed) and `d` on a clean worktree (`y` removed the folder, the branch stayed). The refusal when changes appear after the client looked (REQ-146) is covered only by the unit test of the message. REQ-148 to REQ-150, REQ-157, REQ-158 and REQ-167 by N41: `android/client-core` `ClosePaneTest` (the sheet names what runs, says what stays and what the PC does, a shell at its prompt still asks, no sheet before the document, no PC answers with its message, the selected pane that went is replaced by the first or by none) and `LookTest` (the table equals `contract/themes.json`, the selector shows only the kind in force, each kind keeps its choice, a theme of the other kind is not a choice, `system` follows the phone, what is stored is read back and each value falls back alone); `just android` builds and lints the Compose screens (the sheet, the `×`, the `ajustes` tab and the composer's `imePadding`), which have no UI tests. On a phone against a real daemon the `ajustes` tab, the `×` with its sheet and a real close (3 panes became 2, the next one selected) were seen working; the theme screen, the keyboard raising the field and the skeleton were not seen by me on the phone. REQ-168 is also N42's (`tui.rs` test: a pane whose blocks have not arrived draws bars and not its screen).

REQ-154 by N59: `crates/jwn/src/tui/settings.rs` (`dark_from`: Dark is dark, a failure and an empty or other answer are light, not being able to ask is dark); checked against the real binary with a stand-in `defaults`.

REQ-238 by N58: no automated test (the keyboard's action is Compose's); tried by hand.

REQ-353 to REQ-370 by N80: `crates/jwn/src/agent.rs` tests (the word that names a workspace: id, name, branch, folder, a unique start, several and none; the agent of a pane by its program; the text of a screen, of blocks and of a bracketed paste; the tag; the skill's text and where it installs) and `crates/jwn/tests/agent.rs` through the real binary against a daemon of its own and real git (`ls` and `pane ls` with text and `--json`, `read` of a screen and with `--blocks`, `prompt` into a pane that runs a stand-in for an agent that records what it receives, refusing its own pane, `--now`, an agent that never settles, `worktree` with and without `.jw.toml` and with `--task`, `pane open` with a split and `close` with its refusals, `skill install` into a private folder, the exit codes).

REQ-86 to REQ-89 by N29 (`crates/daemon/tests/mobile.rs` restarts a daemon and finds it again on the same port, `crates/web` tests carry the host, `LinkTest` covers the list of PCs; the list, the green state, connecting, forgetting and the last-used start were seen on the phone against two real daemons, one of them stopped; the lookups are not unit-tested). REQ-83's scan by N28 (`QrTest` decodes frames made from the link, with row padding and inverted; the camera preview and the permission screens were seen on the phone, but a real scan of the terminal's QR was not done by me: the phone lay on the desk). REQ-82, 83, 85 by N27 (`android/client-core` `LinkTest` for the link, pinning, endpoints and denials; the Compose screens and `WsLinkSource` were checked by hand on the phone against a real daemon: pairing over the private address with the pinned certificate, a declined and an accepted pairing, reconnecting with the stored secret, and revoking from the PC; no UI or instrumented tests). REQ-75, REQ-84 and the daemon's half of REQ-80 and REQ-82 by N26 (`crates/daemon/tests/mobile.rs`: a real daemon, a stand-in `cloudflared`, a WebSocket phone that pairs, speaks the daemon's protocol and is refused `mobile_stop`; `src/mobile.rs` tests; `jwn pair`'s prompt and QR were run by hand against the real `cloudflared`, and a WebSocket through the real tunnel reached the bridge: the name took about 80 s to resolve on this machine). REQ-81 by N25 (`crates/web/tests/tls.rs`: a client that pins the fingerprint talks TLS to the listener through a WebSocket, a wrong fingerprint is refused, the certificate is reused after a restart, public and `0.0.0.0` addresses are refused; the phone-side pinning is N27). REQ-80 and REQ-82 (the bridge's half) by N24 (`crates/web/src/tunnel.rs` tests against a stand-in `cloudflared` script, `tests/bridge.rs` for `endpoints`; the real `cloudflared` and its DNS wait are not exercised until N26). REQ-72, 73, 74, 76, 77, 78, 79 by N23 (`crates/web/tests/bridge.rs`: real WebSockets and a stand-in daemon; `src/auth.rs` tests; the 10 ms read tick means a quiet relay costs a wake-up per tick per phone); REQ-75 only through its `confirm` callback until `jwn pair` (N26). REQ-69 to REQ-71 by N20 (`android/client-core` `BlocksTest` for the labels, colours and paths; the Compose screens are built and linted, and were checked by hand on the phone against a real zsh: tap, actions, `■` stopping a `sleep`; Comando and Salida copy were not checked). The phone's `corriendo N s` counts from the daemon's start time against the phone's clock, so a skewed clock shifts it (never below 0). REQ-65 to REQ-68 by N19 (`crates/jwn/src/tui.rs` and `tui/shell.rs` tests on the `snapshot-blocks.json` fixture; the drawing was not tried by eye in a real terminal yet). REQ-61 to REQ-64 by N18 (`crates/jwn/src/tui.rs` tests; typing from both clients, `^C` and the leader checked in the real terminal client and on the phone by `scripts/e2e-live.py`, 39 checks). REQ-54 to REQ-59 and REQ-122 to REQ-127 (split from REQ-54, 57, 59 and 60) by N17 (`crates/daemon/tests/blocks.rs` against real zsh and bash, unit tests of the scanner and the block store; fish was first run in N35, on Linux, see RISK-16; `tests/blocks.rs` also checks REQ-123 and REQ-124: the blocks node keeps its id while the pane lives and goes with it). REQ-48 and REQ-49 by N13 (`crates/daemon/tests/panes.rs` with real `sleep`, `src/procs.rs`, `android/client-core`; checked by hand with `claude`, `nvim` and `sleep` on the phone). REQ-33 to REQ-35 and REQ-39 to REQ-41 by N8 (`android/client-core` tests, `just android`; the Compose screens are built and linted, not UI-tested). REQ-31 and REQ-32 by N6 (`android/contract`, `just android`). REQ-28 to REQ-30 by N5 (`tests/session.rs`, `contract/src/patch.rs` tests, `crates/jwn/tests/e2e.rs`).

REQ-500 to REQ-503 by N94: `crates/daemon/tests/project.rs` against a real daemon and real git (a folder with no settings is one shell; one with a personal file named by its path gets its layout's panes and no setup pane; its own `.jw.toml` is read before the personal file; settings that cannot be used are refused naming the file and the line and start nothing; the top level of a repository has the repository's layout but not its setup, a folder inside it is one shell, and a worktree of it has the setup too), `crates/jwn/src/tui.rs` (a folder is opened through `start_workspace` and nothing fails) and, on the phone, `Data.kt` (compiled and linted: `start_workspace` with one shell as the fallback).
REQ-494 to REQ-499 by N93: `crates/daemon/src/project.rs` tests (names with offsets from 0 to 99, lowercase, digits and `_`; an uppercase or spaced name, an offset of 100, a negative, a string and a fraction each refused naming the line), `crates/daemon/src/model.rs` tests (a block starts at 20000 + 100 × slot, each name is an offset in it, the variables, the lowest free slot with holes filled first and none when the next block would pass 65535, the block saved with the workspace and an old session without one loading), `crates/daemon/tests/project.rs` against a real daemon and real git (the settings list `ports`; two worktrees get the first two blocks and say them; the setup pane of a worktree sees its `<PREFIX>_PORT_BASE` and `<PREFIX>_PORT_WEB`; a change of the settings is for the next worktree; a removed worktree gives its block back and the first one is given again; a project with no `ports` gives none; the blocks survive a restart of the daemon), `crates/jwn/tests/agent.rs` (`jwn worktree` says the ports on its error output), `crates/jwn/src/tui/open.rs` (the box's line) and the contract's fixture with `ports`, in Rust and Kotlin.
REQ-489 to REQ-493 by N92: `crates/daemon/src/provision.rs` tests (a file copied with its folders and only its owner's bits, never over another; a link, a file over 1 MiB, `../`, an absolute path, a path through a link that leads out of the checkout and a worktree folder that is a link are skipped with their reason and nothing is written outside the worktree; the reply says only what there is to say), `crates/daemon/tests/project.rs` against a real daemon and real git (the settings list `copy`, a worktree gets the files at mode 0600 and the reply says what was copied and what was skipped — missing and already there —, the checkout is as it was, nothing to say when there is nothing to copy, and a `copy` that leaves the repository is refused naming the line), `crates/jwn/tests/agent.rs` (`jwn worktree` says it on its error output and prints an id and a path as before), `crates/jwn/src/tui/open.rs` (the box's line) and the contract's fixture with `copy`, in Rust and Kotlin.
REQ-482 to REQ-488 by N91: `crates/daemon/src/project.rs` tests (the personal file named by the origin however it is written — `git@host:o/r.git`, https with user and password, ssh with a port, with spaces — and by the path, with its case, when there is none; an empty origin is none; a `-` and a `/` do not come to one name), `crates/daemon/tests/project.rs` against a real daemon and real git (no file: the defaults and where the personal one would be, with nothing written; the personal file read as a `.jw.toml` is; the repository's wins and the personal one is not read; a worktree has the same personal file; an error in the personal file names it and the line; a worktree starts with the personal setup pane, and with no file there is one shell, no setup pane and nothing created; writing checks first, leaves the personal file at mode 0600 and no temporary file, refuses what does not read and changes nothing, never writes the repository unasked, says when the personal file is hidden by the repository's, and refuses a folder that is no repository), the contract's conformance in Rust and Kotlin (the command's fixture and the reply with `from` and `personal`) and `crates/jwn/src/tui/open.rs` tests for the box's wording; the phone's sheet says the same words, compiled and linted.

The screens were compiled and linted but not seen on a device; the person's phone is installed on only when they say so.
REQ-469 to REQ-478 by N89: `crates/jwn/src/tui/markdown.rs` tests (headings in their colours and listed with their row, a paragraph wrapping by words with a word and what touches it kept together and a long word cut, lists hanging under their text with nesting, tasks and numbers, a quote's bar and code's ground, language and tones, a table with borders cut to the width, and what a copy reads like: the marks left out, broken rows joined, blanks kept), `crates/jwn/src/tui/viewer.rs` tests (the bar and the percent, a line, a page, the ends and the limits, `[` `]` between headings, the headings at the left only when the panel is 100 columns wide and `o`, a click on one, a search with its lit matches, `n` `N` round and a word that is not there, a plan's `1` `2` `3` and a click on an answer, `y`, `e` with and without a file, `esc` and `q`, a control key ignored, a drag with its lit cells and its copy, a press that is a click, a held drag scrolling a line each 60 ms), `crates/jwn/src/tui.rs` tests (`v` on a plan asks for it, the viewer's keys are not the conversation's, `1` answers and closes, an answer to a plan that no longer waits says so and keeps the document, `esc` leaves things as they were, the wheel, a click on an answer, a drag, `y`, `e` opening the plan's file, a `.md` of the tree asking to be read in any case of its extension and any other opening the editor) and `crates/jwn/src/tui/conversation.rs` tests (`v` and a click on the plan's text ask for the viewer). By hand through a pty with a real daemon and the fake engine (a plan of 300 lines, more than the box keeps: it opened with `v`, scrolled to its end, searched for `paso 150`, and `1` answered it with edits allowed), not yet by eye in a window.

By hand through a pty with the fake engine, not yet by eye in a window.

## What is left
In this order, which the user set on 2026-10-15 (before it, on 2026-10-14, he chose that the daily flow of the terminal client comes next, that the special keys of the phone are among the last things and that Markdown comes last; the file tree, which both the viewer and the editor need, is now ahead of them). Each becomes a part (N43 onwards) with its REQs when it starts; a part with a screen starts from an approved prototype. What is missing was found by setting the old jw's README against what `next/` does today.

| # | What |
|---|---|
| 0 | **The conversation (N81 to N86), first** (the user, 2026-10-15): jwn replaces Claude Code's own screen with its own, over the engine's JSON, in the terminal client and on the phone (spec: *The conversation*; prototype `docs/prototypes/conversation.html`; engine `docs/claude-engine.md`). Then `codex` with the same mould (OPEN-11) |
| 1 to 4 | ✓ Spec cleanup, CI for `next/`, the producers of attention (claude's hooks, GitHub) and the parity of closing, the look and pairing a phone from the terminal (N35 to N42). Sessions were decided against for now (RAT-16, OPEN-10) |
| 5 | ✓ `clear`: the command that clears a pane's buffer (N69) |
| 6 | ✓ Sync a worktree: `s` pushes its branch (N70); it does not bring anything from the base (RAT-27) |
| 7 | ✓ Close a worktree: with changes not committed it is removed by force after its branch's name is typed (N71); the branch stays |
| 8 | The panes' layout: ✓ the tree, splitting, going by side and closing (N72); swapping with `H J K L` and resizing with `< > { }` (N73 ✓); dragging a border with the mouse (part 10) |
| 9 | ✓ The history of each pane (its blocks) surviving a daemon restart (N74) |
| 10 | ✓ Selecting and copying with the mouse in the terminal client (N75) |
| 11 | ✓ (N68) Remove the renaming of a workspace that is a folder or a repository's root (it is a directory on disk): only worktrees (their shown name, not their branch or directory) and panes are renamed (decided 2026-10-15), in the terminal client, on the phone and in the daemon |
| 12 | What agents use: ✓ the commands `ls`, `read`, `worktree`, `prompt`, `pane` and `skill install` (N80); `prompt`, `read` and `worktree --task` over the conversation's messages (N86); the review of a pull request (`^␣ R`) still to do, with a prototype first |
| 13 | Projects: ✓ `.jw.toml` for where worktrees go, the setup, what a worktree opens with and, until N96, the branch's template, in the daemon and the terminal client (N78); the phone's sheet reading it (N79 ✓); ports per worktree and `.env` files, and a layout for a folder opened with `o`, later; ✓ the worktrees that exist, listed and opened from the box of `w` (N99), also on the phone |
| 14 | A file tree of the workspace: ✓ the daemon and the terminal client (N76); ✓ the phone's screen «archivos», which lists and opens nothing (N77); what the viewer of Markdown starts from |
| 15 | The viewer of Markdown (N88 to N90, asked for first on 2026-10-16: the plan of Claude opens in it); still left: other text files, a `.md` from a diff, links between documents and the editor (OPEN-9) |
| 16 | Where `esc`, `tab`, the arrows and `^C` go on the phone (OPEN-5) |
| 17 | The swap day: delete the old jw, move `next/` to the root, rename `jwn` to `jw`, decide the Android application id |

**Not to be built** (the user, 2026-10-15): moving between workspaces with numbers, `tab` and the finder, and a header that follows the focused pane's folder and branch: the sidebar as it is is enough.
